---
name: sk-create-readme
description: Author sk-doc folder, code-folder and skill/project READMEs from local evidence.
allowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Grep, Glob]

# Create README (workflow)

`create-readme` is the README authoring packet in the `sk-doc` family. It writes current-state folder READMEs from local evidence, using packet-local templates under `assets/` and shared create-quality-control validators under `../shared`.

---

## 1. WHEN TO USE

### Activation Triggers

Use this packet when the request involves:

- Creating or refreshing a folder `README.md`.
- Documenting a project, skill, feature, component or source-code directory.
- Writing a skill package's README, including the README step `create-skill` hands over while it scaffolds a new skill.
- Writing a repository's front page, contributing guide or security policy.
- Writing developer orientation for a code folder, including topology, boundaries, entrypoints and validation.
- Writing the README of a hook folder, a runtime plugin or extension folder, or a runtime folder and its `SYNC.md`.
- Running `/create:readme`.

Keyword triggers: `create readme`, `/create:readme`, `readme`, `folder readme`, `write readme`, `README.md`, `front-page overview`, `code folder readme`, `skill readme`, `contributing guide`, `security policy`, `sync.md`, `sync doc for the`.

### When NOT to Use

Use another `sk-doc` packet when:

- The user wants to audit, validate, score, or optimize an existing README or another existing document without authoring or refreshing it. Use `create-quality-control`.
- The user wants to scaffold a skill, agent, command, benchmark package, feature catalog, testing playbook, flowchart, or changelog. Use `create-skill`, `create-agent`, `create-command`, `create-benchmark`, `create-feature-catalog`, `create-manual-testing-playbook`, `create-flowchart`, or `create-changelog`.
- The target is not markdown.
- The folder is self-explanatory and a parent README or inline comments already give enough orientation.

This is a nested workflow packet under the `sk-doc` parent hub. It owns README authoring only. It does not carry `graph-metadata.json`; advisor identity, skill graph metadata and cross-packet routing live at the `sk-doc` hub root.

---

## 2. SMART ROUTING

`sk-doc` routes README authoring here: the request names a README, a folder readme, a code-folder readme or `/create:readme`.
The hub's mode signal selects this packet.
The artifact decides the template before the folder decides the sections.

Authoring starts from the matching template: `assets/readme-template.md` for a repository front page or a general README, `assets/skill-readme-template.md` for a skill package README, `assets/readme-code-template.md` for a source-code folder and `assets/architecture-template.md` for a skill package's `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
A repository's root guides start from `assets/contributing-template.md` and `assets/security-policy-template.md`.
Hook code starts from its own scaffolds: `assets/hook-readme-template.md` for a shared hook folder and its runtime adapter folders, `assets/runtime-plugin-readme-template.md` for a folder a runtime loads its hooks from and `assets/runtime-folder-template.md` for a runtime folder's README and `SYNC.md`.
`references/README.md` is the fallback route map when the artifact type or folder purpose is unclear.

When nothing matches, report `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK` with the router's disambiguation checklist instead of guessing.

Read [`ROUTER.md`](./ROUTER.md) before selecting skill-local resources; open only the matched files.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Every run follows one operating model, whatever the artifact:

1. Read local evidence first: the target folder, nearby docs, package files, config files and existing commands. Never document unconfirmed files, commands, APIs or metrics.
2. Route by artifact type and folder purpose (`ROUTER.md` Section 3) to pick the output and template.
3. Draft current-state content only, in the smallest useful shape: a general README (Section 5) or a code-folder README (Section 6).
4. Copy the matching template as a scaffold, remove unused sections and put orientation first.
5. Validate the authored markdown and resolve local links before delivery (Section 7).

Both README shapes share this lifecycle. The sections below give the detailed steps for each.

---

## 4. README AUTHORING WORKFLOW

1. Identify the target folder, audience and README type before drafting.
2. Read the target folder contents, nearby documentation, package files, config files and existing commands.
3. Do not invent commands, files, APIs, metrics, features or capabilities.
4. Choose the smallest useful README shape. Use a general README for project, skill, feature and component roots. Use a code-folder README for source-directory orientation.
5. Write current-state documentation only. Use stable file paths, commands, feature names and source links instead of packet IDs, phase IDs, migration history or temporary planning labels.
6. Put orientation first: what this is, who it is for and how to use or navigate it.
7. Apply progressive disclosure: title and tagline answer "what is this", overview helps the reader decide relevance, quick start appears only when there is a runnable path and detailed reference comes later.
8. Use two-tier voice for project, skill and feature READMEs: plain narrative for overview and usage, precise reference tables for options, configuration and tool signatures.
9. Use technical reference voice for component and code-folder READMEs.
10. Copy the relevant template only as a scaffold. Remove unused sections instead of leaving placeholders.
11. Use relative links for local documents and verify that links resolve from the README location.
12. Test documented commands when feasible. If a command cannot be tested locally, mark it clearly as an example.
13. Show expected output for verification commands.
14. Validate the authored markdown before delivery.

---

## 5. GENERAL README OUTPUT SHAPE

A general README answers two questions: what this is, and how someone uses or navigates it.

Use only the sections that fit the audience.

| Section | Include When | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Overview | Always | Purpose, audience, main value and prerequisites |
| Key Statistics | Useful facts help readers decide scope | Counts, version, supported modes or limits |
| How This Compares | Readers may confuse this with nearby tools | Short comparison table |
| Quick Start | Readers need to run or try something | Short setup, verification and first use |
| Features | Capabilities need explanation | Feature groups, behavior notes and examples |
| Requirements | Runtime, tool or service needs exist | Minimum versions, permissions and external services |
| Structure | Navigation matters | Directory tree and key files |
| Configuration | Settings exist | Config files, options, defaults and env vars |
| Usage Examples | Patterns are easier shown than described | Simple and advanced examples with expected results |
| Troubleshooting | Users can hit known issues | What they see, cause and fix |
| FAQ | Repeated questions exist | One heading per question, short answers only |
| Related Resources | Links exist | Grouped skills, documents, commands or external references |

General README format rules:

- H1 is a plain title.
- Add a one-sentence blockquote tagline immediately after the H1. A repository front page may put its pitch under the nav line of its first section instead.
- H2 headings are numbered and ALL CAPS, for example `## 1. OVERVIEW`.
- Numbered H3 and H4 subsections inside feature sections are ALL CAPS, for example `### 3.1 TECHNICAL REFERENCE`.
- Unnumbered H3 and H4 headings use Title Case.

- Unnumbered H3 and H4 headings use Title Case.
- Name the feature or artifact in its heading: `### Spec Kit: a written record of every change`, not `### Powered by Spec Kit`.
- Emoji use follows two registers. The repository front-page README may use decorative emoji on section headings. Every other README uses semantic emoji only (✅ ❌ ⚠️ 🔒 🚨) for rules and status markers.
- Give every item in an inventory a description. A list of bare names says nothing about when to use each one.
- Explain in short paragraphs of short sentences, about 10-15 words each and about 60 words a paragraph. Use bullets only for items a reader scans or compares: commands, options, steps and parallel facts. A run of bullets that each hold a sentence of explanation reads better as a paragraph.
- When installing takes more than a few steps, a front-page README keeps only the minimum commands and points to a root install document, such as `INSTALL.md`.
- A front-page README follows the order in `assets/readme-template.md` Section 4: a nav line under the first H2, a highlights list, install and then how it works. Each FAQ question is its own heading.
- Use `---` horizontal rules between H2 sections. A repository's root guides put one `&nbsp;` line above each H2 instead. Only the guides the validator exempts by name may do so, and the selection guide in `assets/readme-template.md` lists them.
- Do not add a Table of Contents.
- Do not add `<!-- ANCHOR -->` navigation comments.
- Use tables for comparisons, file maps, configuration, options and troubleshooting.
- Use fenced code blocks with language tags.

Frontmatter is optional for normal project READMEs and recommended for skill or system documentation that should be discoverable.

```yaml
---
title: "[DOCUMENT_TITLE]"
description: "[One-sentence description]"
trigger_phrases:
  - "[search phrase]"
  - "[routing phrase]"
---
```

---

## 6. CODE-FOLDER README OUTPUT SHAPE

Create a code-folder README when a developer needs local orientation before editing the directory.

Include details when relevant:

- High-level purpose: what the folder owns.
- Architecture diagram: how callers, handlers, adapters and storage relate.
- Package topology: layers, zones and allowed dependency direction.
- Directory tree with only important files.
- Key files and responsibilities.
- Boundaries: what this folder may import, export or own.
- Data or control flow through important paths.
- Public entrypoints, exports or commands.
- Validation and test commands.
- Related documents.

Use this section model:

| Section | Include When | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Overview | Always | One or two paragraphs about current responsibility |
| Architecture Diagram | Helpful for dispatch, pipelines, adapters or layered flows | Prefer zones and arrows over prose |
| Package Topology | The folder has layers, zones or import rules | Show dependency direction clearly |
| Directory Tree | Always for multi-file folders | Show only important files |
| Key Files | Responsibilities are split | Table with file and role |
| Boundaries | Import rules or ownership matter | State allowed and disallowed edges |
| Data or Control Flow | Runtime paths matter | Keep to the main path |
| Entrypoints | The folder exposes APIs, commands or exports | Include function names, files or CLI commands |
| Validation | Tests or checks exist | Use commands that work from repo root |
| Related | Nearby docs exist | Link parent, sibling and architecture docs |

The general README format rules at `General README format rules:` above bind code-folder READMEs.
That covers numbered H2 headings, ALL-CAPS H2 casing, `---` separators and language-tagged fences.
It also covers no Table of Contents and no anchor-comment navigation.
The blockquote tagline is the exception.
It does not bind code-folder READMEs.
This is a cross-reference to the single authoritative rule block, not a second list.

The Directory Tree rule is shape-conditional and script-implementable:

1. Count the target folder's immediate subdirectories.
2. When that count is greater than zero, include a fenced Directory Tree because the folder has nesting or layering. The validator emits `code_folder_directory_tree` when it is missing.
3. When that count is zero, a complete `CONTENTS`, `FILES`, `KEY FILES`, `WHAT IS IN THE PACKAGE` or `STRUCTURE` inventory naming every direct file other than the README satisfies the navigation requirement. A file named in a fenced structure tree counts. The validator emits `code_folder_flat_inventory` when that table is absent or incomplete.

Both branches are named rules. A reviewer must not replace the subdirectory-count condition with a judgement about whether a tree feels useful.

Code-folder README rules:

- Document current behavior only.
- Use code terms exactly as they appear in files.
- Prefer file paths, exported names and commands over narrative history.
- Keep diagrams small enough to read in a terminal.
- State allowed dependency direction when the folder has layers.
- Do not force Quick Start, Features, FAQ or Troubleshooting unless the directory has runnable commands, meaningful capabilities or known reader problems.
- The code-folder scaffold omits the general blockquote tagline by design. Frontmatter remains optional, and when present it stays before the H1.

---

## 7. VALIDATION

Run shared validation on authored markdown when feasible:

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/check_authored_name_kebab.py <artifact-path-or-slug>
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/validate_document.py <path>
```

Run the authored-name check after resolving the output path and before document validation. `README.md` is a tool-mandated exemption; every other authored name must pass the kebab-case rule.

Use quick validation or structure extraction when appropriate:

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/quick_validate.py <path>
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/extract_structure.py <path>
```

As an internal authoring check after creating or refreshing READMEs, the packet-local inventory script can verify repository coverage from the repo root:

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-readme/scripts/audit_readmes.py --repo-root . --validator .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/validate_document.py
```

Use `--json-out`, `--markdown-out` or `--inventory-out` when the user needs artifacts. Treat broken references, missing key artifact coverage and validator blocking errors as findings to fix or report.

Pre-publish checks for READMEs:

- README type is correct. Code folders use the code-folder shape.
- Included sections match the audience and task.
- No empty sections or placeholders remain.
- Section numbers are sequential.
- Local links use correct relative paths.
- Commands were tested or clearly marked as examples.
- Expected outputs are shown for verification commands.
- Code blocks specify language tags.
- HVR passes.

---

## 8. WRITING RULES

### ALWAYS

- Read the target folder and nearby docs before writing.
- Choose the README type before choosing a template.
- Keep READMEs current-state and reader-focused.
- Use relative links for local documents.
- Use fenced code blocks with language tags.
- Test documented commands when feasible.
- Show expected output for verification commands.
- Use tables for file maps, options, comparisons and troubleshooting.
- Keep `README.md` and `SKILL.md` casing intact.

### NEVER

- Add a Table of Contents or anchor-comment navigation.
- Leave template placeholders in the final document.
- Document commands, files or features not confirmed from workspace or user evidence.
- Cite mutable packet numbers, phase IDs or migration bookkeeping in durable README content.
- Force Quick Start, Features, FAQ or Troubleshooting into a code-folder README when the folder only needs orientation.
- Create `graph-metadata.json` in this packet.

### HUMAN VOICE RULES

- No em dashes. Use commas, periods or colons.
- No semicolons. Split the sentence or use "and".
- No Oxford comma in inline lists.
- No setup phrases.
- No banned words from the HVR reference.
- Prefer active voice and direct verbs.
- Use analogies only when they clarify narrative sections. Do not use analogies in component or code-folder READMEs.

### ESCALATE IF

- The target folder purpose or audience is unclear.
- The README would require product, brand or public-facing claims that are not in the repo.
- Documenting a step needs secrets, paid services, destructive operations or external accounts.
- Validation fails in a way that requires changing code or configuration outside the requested document.

---

## 9. OVERFLOW REFERENCES

The core workflow lives in this `SKILL.md`. Use these files only for deep overflow detail, exhaustive scaffolds, edge cases and long examples:

- `assets/readme-template.md` for the selection guide, the general README scaffold and the front-page scaffold.
- `assets/skill-readme-template.md` for a skill package README, the template `create-skill` hands the README step to.
- `assets/contributing-template.md` and `assets/security-policy-template.md` for a repository's root guides.
- `assets/readme-code-template.md` for the full fillable code-folder scaffold and diagram examples.
- `assets/hook-readme-template.md`, `assets/runtime-plugin-readme-template.md` and `assets/runtime-folder-template.md` for hook folders, the runtime folders that load them and a runtime folder's README and `SYNC.md`.
- `assets/architecture-template.md` for a skill package's `ARCHITECTURE.md`: the fixed eight-section system document.
- `references/README.md` for the overflow route map that indexes the `readme/` reference group.
- `references/readme/` for extended README type, voice, writing-pattern and quality detail across three single-concern files.
- `../shared/references/core-standards.md` for shared document formatting rules.
- `../sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md` for the full Human Voice Rules.
- `../shared/references/evergreen-packet-id-rule.md` for current-state documentation rules.

[0m$ [0msed -n '1,140p' .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-changelog/SKILL.md
---
name: sk-create-changelog
description: Author global component changelogs in the v4 narrative style, with placement, versioning, lean content and release notes.
allowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Grep, Glob]
version: 1.6.0.0
---

# create-changelog

`create-changelog` is the changelog-authoring workflow packet of the `sk-doc` family. It writes a global component changelog under `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`. Packet-local (nested) changelogs under a spec packet's `changelog/` folder are deprecated, and this workflow no longer writes them (section 4).

This file is the executable contract: resolve the source, resolve the global target, calculate the version, generate, validate and write. `assets/changelog-template.md` owns what an entry says and how it reads. The `references/` set, routed by `references/README.md`, holds worked examples and edge cases.

---

## 1. WHEN TO USE

### Activation Triggers

Use this workflow to:

1. Create a global component changelog at `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`.
2. Answer a request for a packet-local (nested) changelog: refuse it as deprecated and offer a global component entry instead.
3. Run or support `/create:changelog`.
4. Resolve a changed spec folder, component hint or recent git history into a changelog target.
5. Calculate the next four-part version for a global changelog.
6. Prepare GitHub release notes from a generated global changelog.

Keyword triggers: `create changelog`, `/create:changelog`, `changelog`, `changelog entry`, `release notes`, `global changelog`, `packet-local changelog`, `nested changelog`, `since the last version`, `vX.Y.Z.B`.

### When NOT to Use

1. A release plan with no file output.
2. A README (`create-readme`), agent, command, benchmark, feature catalog, manual testing playbook or skill. Each has its own `create-*` packet. A flowchart or any other diagram belongs to the `sk-design` hub (`/design:diagram`), which this hub does not register a packet for.
3. Auditing, scoring or optimizing an existing changelog (`create-quality-control`).
4. A source too ambiguous to resolve to a spec folder, a component hint or recent git history.
5. Branch, commit or PR work and release mechanics beyond `--release`, which `sk-git` owns. The tag and GitHub release run in the command YAMLs' release step (section 8), and only for the `skilled` release line.

---

## 2. SMART ROUTING

The `sk-doc` hub resolves a changelog-authoring request through `mode-registry.json`.
`hub-router.json` scores the request, the winning `workflowMode` is `sk-create-changelog`, and the hub loads this `SKILL.md` first.
A request to create a global component changelog, write release notes, calculate the next four-part version or resolve a changed source selects this packet.
A packet-local changelog request that lands here is refused as deprecated and offered a global entry.
The packet has one output mode, the global component entry.

`references/README.md` is the route map and the fallback load, and `assets/changelog-template.md` owns what an entry says and how it reads.

A request that matches no registered mode returns `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`, never a silent default.

Read [`ROUTER.md`](./ROUTER.md) before selecting skill-local resources; open only the matched files.

---

## 3. REQUIRED INPUTS

| Input | Required | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `source_type` | Yes, resolved before writing | `spec_folder`, `component` or `git_history` |
| `spec_folder` | When `source_type = spec_folder` | The spec folder to summarize into a global component entry |
| `component_hint` | When `source_type = component` | A name matched against the discovered changelog folders |
| `version_bump` | Optional, default `auto` | `major`, `minor`, `patch`, `build` or `auto` |
| `--nested` | Refused | Packet-local changelogs are deprecated. The workflow stops and offers a global entry (section 4) |
| `--release` | Optional | After a `skilled` release entry is written, tags the version and publishes the GitHub release with the entry as its body (section 8). Any other component skips the release |

Global output lives under `.skilled/changelog/`. Versions read `v{MAJOR}.{MINOR}.{PATCH}.{BUILD}`, the first one is `v1.0.0.0` and dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`. Between 40 and 79 percent confidence in the source or target, proceed and record the assumptions. Below 40 percent, stop and ask with A/B/C options.

---

## 4. HOW IT WORKS: CHANGELOG OUTPUT MODES

### Global Component Changelog

A global entry is written to `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`.
`{component}` is a lowercase kebab-case segment matching `^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, or `{hub}/{link}` when the folder is a hub, with each segment matching that pattern.
Reject an ambiguous hint rather than emit a guessed or underscore-bearing directory.
The `v{VERSION}.md` filename is an exact version contract and is never slug-normalized.

| Bump | Calculation | Source trigger intent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `major` | `{MAJOR+1}.0.0.0` | Breaking, overhaul, rewrite, migration, platform version jump |
| `minor` | `{MAJOR}.{MINOR+1}.0.0` | Significant new feature or subsystem addition |
| `patch` | `{MAJOR}.{MINOR}.{PATCH+1}.0` | Incremental improvement, bug fix, refactor, docs, cleanup |
| `build` | `{MAJOR}.{MINOR}.{PATCH}.{BUILD+1}` | Hotfix, typo, same-day build on an already-published version |

Auto detection takes the explicit `--bump` flag first, then change-type keywords in the `spec.md` title and purpose, then conventional commit prefixes. With no clear signal it defaults to `patch`. If the calculated file already exists, increment the build segment until the path is unique.

### Packet-Local Changelogs (Deprecated)

Spec folders no longer get changelogs. Changelogs live only under `.skilled/changelog/` and in each skill's own `changelog/` folder. This workflow writes no file under `specs/**/changelog/` and refuses `--nested`.

Existing packet-local files such as `changelog-<packet>-root.md` stay where they are and still validate. The spec-kit nested generator that rendered them is removed.

---

## 5. CHANGELOG FORMAT CONTRACT

Read `assets/changelog-template.md` before generating global content. It owns the compact and expanded shapes (section 2), what to leave out and the say-it-once rule (section 3), voice and length (section 4) and format selection (section 5). Every entry must read in the layout of `.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.1.0.0.md`, at whatever length the template's ceilings allow. That entry also models sentence length, plain words, feature detail and the testing section. `.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.1.0.md` models selection, and `.skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.0.md` models voice and how a major release opens. Neither of those two is a layout model.

The contract in one pass:

1. Frontmatter first, then an optional editorial title H1, then the opening. No version header, backlink or version-date line.
2. Compact under 10 reader-visible changes when the release is neither major nor breaking: a one-paragraph summary, `## What's New at a Glance` as short bullets and `## Upgrade`, with `## Why This Release` only when the summary does not say why.
3. Expanded for 10 or more changes, a major bump or any breaking change:
   - an opening of two to four lead sentences, each with short bullets, then a `> [!IMPORTANT]` box listing every break.
   - `## Why This Release`, then `## What's New at a Glance` as a table of Change, What It Means and Section.
   - topical H2 sections, each opened by one or two short paragraphs, with benefit-led H4 items of short bullets.
   - each H4 ending in an `<a id="c-{item-slug}"></a>` anchor the glance table links to.
   - `> [!WARNING]` boxes where a change breaks something and a folded Smaller fixes block last in a section.
   - `## How It Was Checked` with its costs and tests folded, and `## Upgrade Notes` with numbered steps and folded groups.
4. A spec-folder blockquote, ``> Spec folder: `{path}` ``, when the release has a spec folder. It closes the entry, after the last section and an `&nbsp;` line. It carries the path only, and the level stays in the packet. Several folders fold into a `<details>` block headed `Spec folders ({N})`.
5. Content chosen by reader impact and each fact said once. No file or schema tables unless the numbers are the story.
6. Detail by default, with no request needed. Each new feature says how it works and shows a real line of output. Every fix a reader could run into is named. `## How It Was Checked` gives the before and after, the cost and the tests that ran.
7. Short sentences in plain words: about 8 words each, none past 22 and 10 at most on average. Short bullets outnumber paragraphs.
8. One source line per paragraph, bullet and alert line, because a GitHub release page turns every newline into a line break.

When a source such as an older command snippet conflicts with the template, follow the template, write the narrative format and record the mismatch rather than inventing a hybrid.

### Frontmatter Contract

A global entry must be as findable as a spec document, so its frontmatter block carries these five keys, in this order:

| Key | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `title` | `<component> v<version>, <editorial title>`, or `<component> v<version>` when the entry has no editorial title. A Skilled release entry uses its H1, or `Skilled v<version>` |
| `description` | One or two plain sentences, 250 characters at most, saying what the release changed |
| `trigger_phrases` | The identity phrases first, then one or two topic phrases |
| `importance_tier` | `"normal"` |
| `contextType` | `"general"` |

Identity phrases name the entry the way a reader asks for it.
A skill entry declares `<component> v<version>` and `<component> <version>`, where `<component>` is the `name:` in the `SKILL.md` of the folder that holds the entry's changelog folder, never a `{hub}/{link}` target.
The deep-loop runtime has no `SKILL.md` and is `deep-loop-runtime`.
A Skilled release entry declares `v<version> release notes` and `skilled v<version>`.
A topic phrase is 2 to 6 words from the entry's own text that name what changed.
It carries no version number and never fits any release the way "bug fixes" would.
A component entry may keep a `version:` key after the five.
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0msed -n '140,320p' .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-changelog/SKILL.md
Existing packet-local entries keep the block the removed nested generator rendered, with an identity phrase derived from the output path.

---

## 6. TOPOLOGY AND TARGET RESOLUTION

### Mode Detection

For a spec folder, read `implementation-summary.md` first and `spec.md` for the title, purpose and level. Read `tasks.md` only to confirm what shipped. Every source resolves to global mode:

1. `--nested` present: stop, say packet-local changelogs are deprecated and offer a global entry.
2. A spec folder, including a phase child, a phase parent or a folder that already has `changelog/`, resolves to the global component its changed paths touch (Global Component Discovery below).
3. A component hint or git history source is built from recent commits and the files they touched.

### Global Component Discovery

Discover component folders from the repository at run time with `ls -d .skilled/changelog/*/ 2>/dev/null | sort`, never from a hardcoded table. Then resolve:

1. Each folder is a plain component name, such as `sk-doc` or `system-spec-kit`, with no numeric prefix. One folder is not a skill: `skilled` is the framework release line, with one entry per Skilled release.
2. Match changed paths against folder names by whole path segment, never by substring.
3. Match `.skilled/skills/{name}/**` to the folder named `{name}`, and `.skilled/commands/**` or `.skilled/agents/**` to the folder that owns them.
4. Resolve a hub to one of its links, never to the hub folder.
   A hub is a real folder other than `skilled`, such as `sk-doc` or `sk-code`, holding one link per member packet plus `parent`, the hub's own changelog.
   `ls -l .skilled/changelog/{hub}/` lists them.
   A path under `.skilled/skills/{hub}/{packet}/` resolves to the link whose target is `{packet}/changelog`.
   Any other path under the hub, and a hint that names the hub, resolve to `{hub}/parent`.
   A hint that names a member by its link, its packet folder or `{hub}/{link}` resolves to that link.
5. Match `component_hint` against folder names exactly.
6. Use spec path segments as a tiebreaker.
7. Resolve to `skilled` only when `component_hint` names it. A changed path never selects it, because the operator chooses a framework release.
8. If nothing matches, do not invent or default a folder. Pause and ask which component the changelog belongs to.

Then choose the target:

1. A component with more than 60 percent of the changed files is primary.
2. With several roughly equal components, pick the primary by file count and list the rest as secondary.
3. Verify the target folder exists before writing, and never create a missing one.

---

## 7. CREATION WORKFLOW

Complete these seven steps in order. The command YAMLs number the same workflow as eight steps, because they run the optional GitHub release as its own step 7.

Before writing, validate each authored component, packet or phase slug. Exact version filenames and frozen changelog paths stay exempt.

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/shared/scripts/check_authored_name_kebab.py <component-or-phase-slug>
```

1. **Analyze context.** Resolve the source type and read the source (section 6). Compile `work_context`: what changed for the reader, the change type, the level and the source.
2. **Resolve output target.** Discover the folders, match paths and hints (section 6), choose the primary component, list secondary ones and verify `.skilled/changelog/{resolved_folder}/` exists.
3. **Determine version.** Do these in order:
   - List the `v*.md` entries in the target folder and in its generation folders (`v1+/`, `v2+/` and so on).
   - List the version entries on the upstream branch with `git ls-tree`, which reads the branch's upstream, else `origin/main`.
   For the `skilled` release line with at least one entry, first check the highest entry for a release tag with `git ls-remote --exit-code --tags origin refs/tags/v{latest}`:
   - Exit 2 means no tag, so that release is still open. Extend that entry wherever it sits instead of numbering a new one, keep its version and skip the bump.
   - A bump the operator chose is not applied, and the report says so, and says that tagging the open release first starts a new one.
   - When the open entry exists only upstream, stop and ask for a merge or rebase first.
   - Any exit other than 0 or 2 means the check failed, so number a new entry and say so rather than risk editing a release that already shipped.
   Every other component numbers a new entry.
   - Start from the highest of the local and upstream `vX.Y.Z.B` versions, choose the bump (section 4) and calculate the next version.
   - Increment the build segment while the file exists locally or upstream, because two branches numbering from their own checkout pick the same version and collide at merge.
   - Without a reachable upstream, number locally and say so in the report.
4. **Generate content.** Read `assets/changelog-template.md`, then:
   - Write the frontmatter block per the Frontmatter Contract in section 5.
   - Choose the content before writing it (template section 3). Start from what the reader would notice, act on or decide differently about, confirm each against the source, merge items with the same effect and drop the housekeeping.
   - Select compact or expanded from the changes that survive (template section 5).
   - Write each section to its one job, so a fact appears once: themes in the opening, the gap in Why This Release, one payoff line per theme at a glance, the detail in the H4 items and only actions in the upgrade notes.
   - Explain how each new feature works, step by step, and quote one real line of its output. Name every fix a reader could run into.
   - Write `## How It Was Checked` from evidence the source records: the before and after of a real command, the measured cost and each suite's result. Run the command when the source lacks its output. Never write a number that no run produced, and say so when something was not measured.
   - Write short sentences in everyday words (template section 4). Split a long sentence rather than cut its fact.
   - Lead with why the release matters. Beyond the glance table and the before-and-after table, add a table only when its numbers are the story.
   - Order the opening, the glance list, the topical sections and the upgrade notes by what matters most to the reader, never by when the work landed.
   - When extending an open release, read the entry first and fold the new work into it.
     Add its section or H4 items with their anchors, a glance row in section order, any break to the important box, its spec folder to the spec-folder list and any upgrade action.
     Widen the opening and Why This Release only enough to cover the new theme, keep the title unless the new work changes the headline, and correct any existing sentence the new work makes untrue.
     When the new work matters more to a reader than what the entry leads with, retitle the entry and re-rank its sections, so the release still opens on its most important change.
5. **Validate quality.** Run the section 9 checks on the draft. For a new entry, confirm the version is strictly greater than the latest one and no file exists at the target. For an extension, confirm the target is the open entry. Confirm every path the entry names exists. Apply the cut test (template section 3), fix what is safe and revalidate.
6. **Write the file.** Write `.skilled/changelog/{primary_component}/v{next_version}.md`, or edit the open entry in place when extending, then read back its first lines.
   Set `next_version` in every version carrier the component's skill root already has (`SKILL.md`, `description.json`, `hub-router.json` and `mode-registry.json`), never in `README.md` or a hub `ROUTER.md`, whose versions are each document's own.
   For a hub, run `node .skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs` on the hub and stop on a non-zero exit.
   Report secondary components as candidates rather than writing extra files.
7. **Report and preserve context.** Report the path, component, version, whether the entry is new or extended, bump type, summary, section count and H4 item count. For a `skilled` entry, also report the release: its URL when one is published, or that none is and the `--release` run that publishes it. When the entry belongs to a published release, bring the release in step first (section 8). A spec-folder source can preserve context through the normal memory save workflow. Do not claim completion before the written file is verified.

Pause when confidence drops below 40 percent, component resolution stays ambiguous, the calculated version looks wrong or a required section would be empty.

---

## 8. NOTATION AND FORMAT RULES

The voice, structure, omission and length rules live in `assets/changelog-template.md` sections 3 and 4. The non-negotiables are:

1. Write for a smart person who is not a developer, and lead with why the release matters.
2. Keep what the reader would notice or act on, how each feature works, every fix and the evidence in How It Was Checked. Drop file inventories, test counts outside that section, internal labels, process detail and follow-on housekeeping, and compress reverted work to one story sentence or nothing.
3. Say each fact once, in the section whose job it is.
4. In an expanded entry:
   - a glance table whose Section column names each topical H2, in section order.
   - H4 item headings of 2-7 words (10 at most), each ending in an `<a id="c-{item-slug}"></a>` anchor.
   - `> [!WARNING]` boxes where a break is described, and one `> [!IMPORTANT]` box listing every break.
   - folded `<details>` blocks with a `<summary><b>…</b></summary>` header, the only bold text.
   - an `&nbsp;` line before every H2 and before the closing spec-folder block, and no `---` rule in the body.
5. No Oxford commas, em dashes or semicolons, per the Human Voice Rules.
6. Short sentences in everyday words. No sentence runs past 22 words and the entry averages 10 at most.

### Release Notes

For GitHub release notes, use the changelog content with any YAML frontmatter and the editorial title H1 removed, keeping its spacing, then append an `&nbsp;` line and the pointer. `scripts/build_release_notes.py <entry> --out <file>` builds exactly this body, so a publish and every later amend match the entry:

```text
&nbsp;

Full changelog: `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`
```

The command YAMLs own the release itself, as `step_7_publish_release`.
They run it only when `publish_release` is explicitly true and the component is `skilled`, the framework release line.
Any other component skips the release, because component versions share the `vX.X.X.X` shape with release tags and a component version must never become one.
The tag is `v{next_version}`, and the release title is the tag followed by the entry's editorial title, the H1 without its leading version.
The step checks for a tag collision and an authenticated `gh`, then runs `git tag -a`, `git push origin {release_tag}` and `gh release create {release_tag} --title {release_title} --notes-file {notes_file}`, which publishes at once with no draft stage.
`:confirm` shows the exact commands and runs them only after approval.

#### Keep a Published Release in Step

A published release must always show its entry as it reads now. Whenever an entry changes after its release is published, bring the release in step in the same run, without waiting to be asked:

1. Rebuild the body with `build_release_notes.py`.
2. Run `gh release edit {release_tag} --notes-file {notes_file}`, adding `--title {release_title}` when the editorial title changed.
3. Check the tag: `git show {release_tag}:{entry_path}` must match the entry on the default branch.
4. Report the release URL from `gh release view {release_tag} --json url`.

When step 3 shows a difference, the tag points at a commit that holds older text or older code. Recommend a republish, so the tag, the entry and the release agree.

Republishing deletes and recreates a public ref, so it needs the operator's yes each time. Never run it as a default or in an unattended run. Ask once, in the same reply as the report, with republish as the recommended answer. Name the old tag object and say it can be pushed back. Only a request that names the tag move counts as the yes, such as "republish the tag" or "move the tag". A request to publish or edit the notes does not. The body edit in step 2 needs no such wait.

After the yes, run these commands in order. The local tag goes too, or `git tag -a` fails because it still exists:

```bash
gh release delete {release_tag} --cleanup-tag --yes
git tag -d {release_tag}
git tag -a {release_tag} -m "{tag_message}"
git push origin {release_tag}
gh release create {release_tag} --title "{release_title}" --notes-file {notes_file} --latest --verify-tag
```

---

## 9. VALIDATION

Validate the target, version and content before writing.

Global changelog checks:

1. The target is `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md` in an existing folder, and no file exists at that path. Extending an open `skilled` release is the one exception: the target is that release's existing entry.
2. The version follows `vX.Y.Z.B` and is strictly greater than the latest one in the folder, counting its generation folders. An extension keeps the open release's version.
3. The frontmatter carries `title`, `description`, `trigger_phrases`, `importance_tier` and `contextType` in contract order, with both identity phrases for the target path and at least one topic phrase. `validate_document.py` blocks an entry whose block, keys or phrases are missing.
4. The prose opens with the summary or the opening lead sentences, and the spec-folder blockquote closes the entry when the source is a spec folder, after the last section and an `&nbsp;` line.
5. The tier's sections are present: compact carries the summary, `## What's New at a Glance` and `## Upgrade`. Expanded carries the opening narrative, `## Why This Release`, `## What's New at a Glance`, at least one topical H2 with H4 items, `## How It Was Checked` and `## Upgrade Notes`.
6. No Files Changed, Test Impact or Schema Changes section. Any table outside How It Was Checked passes the earned-evidence rule.
7. Omission applied: no file inventories, process detail or housekeeping items, and no test counts outside How It Was Checked. No internal labels (task, requirement, scenario or finding IDs, packet or phase numbers) appear outside the spec-folder line.
8. Each fact appears once: no sentence restates one from another section, and each glance row adds something the opening did not.
9. The template's ceilings hold: opening within 5 parts, prose near its word budgets (guidance, counted in words, never sentences), glance rows one line each and H4 headings within 10 words. Every paragraph and bullet is one source line. No line, H4 item or glance row ceiling applies.
10. H4 headings are benefit-led and never numbered or sentence-length, and no H3 sits inside a topical section.
11. `scripts/check_changelog_shape.py` passes the draft. It fails glance rows or tagged glance bullets out of section order, a spec-folder line with a level label and a spec-folder line that does not close the entry after an `&nbsp;` line. It also fails a sentence past 22 words, an average past 10 words a sentence and an expanded entry with no `## How It Was Checked`.

Voice gate, run on the draft before writing:

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/scripts/hvr_scan.py <draft-file>
```

The scan must report zero hard blockers (banned punctuation or words).
Deductions below that threshold are acceptable.
If the scanner is unavailable, apply the rules by hand and record that the scan was skipped.
Structural checks complement it:
- a glance table with a Section column, and item anchors its links resolve to.
- an `&nbsp;` line before every H2.
- no bold text outside a folded block's summary header.
- no `---` rule in the body.
- no `**Problem:**`/`**Fix:**` labels.

---
title: "system-deep-loop"
description: "The advisor-routable hub for the active deep-loop workflow families over a nested runtime/ layer."

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Reach for the hub when a request needs more than one round. Use the research mode for a topic that spans several sources, where each round should shape the next query. Use the review mode for a multi-pass audit of a spec folder or a file set. Use it for a release-readiness verdict. Both modes fit an unattended run, because each keeps its state on disk and checks convergence after every pass.

Use `@context` for a one-shot lookup and `/speckit:plan` for implementation planning. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`. A planning council is a separate agent, reached through `/speckit:ai-council`. It is not a mode of this hub.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec packets, validation and memory continuity that both modes read and write. |
| `sk-code` | Its code-review mode handles a single-pass review. The `deep-review/` packet runs the multi-pass loop. |

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The compiled route returns `"action":"defer"` | The prompt names no mode, because `routerPolicy.defaultMode` is `null` | Add a mode word such as `deep research` or `deep review`. A mode hint works too. |
| The loop did not start | The mode was dispatched outside its command, so no workflow owned the state | Run `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. The command YAML owns state, dispatch and convergence. |
| The drift check reports `registry packet absent from hub index` | This README no longer names a packet folder that `mode-registry.json` registers | Add the folder name with a trailing slash to the README |

---

## 6. VERIFICATION

The parent-skill check validates the registry shape, the tool-surface union, the hub-router consistency and the ROUTER contract in one pass.

```bash
node .skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop
```

Expected output: `PASS:` lines for the checked invariants, with `INFO:` lines where the hub declares no surface packets. The summary reads `all hard invariants passed, 0 warnings`. Exit code 0.

```bash
node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/check-documentation-drift.cjs
```

Expected output: a JSON report whose `errors` array is empty. Exit code 0. The check confirms that every registered packet appears in this README with a trailing slash and that every benchmark report folder is indexed. It also fails on a broken relative link in the hub, packet, runtime and report docs.

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/scripts/validate_document.py .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md --type readme
```

Expected output: `✅ VALID` and `Total issues: 0`.

# Skilled - Spec-Driven Agent Loops

[![GitHub Stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops?style=for-the-badge&logo=github&color=fce566&labelColor=222222)](https://github.com/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops/stargazers)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/github/license/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops?style=for-the-badge&color=7bd88f&labelColor=222222)](LICENSE)
[![Latest Release](https://img.shields.io/github/v/release/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops?style=for-the-badge&color=5ad4e6&labelColor=222222)](https://github.com/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops/releases)

> Like it? [buymeacoffee.com/michelkerkmeester](https://buymeacoffee.com/michelkerkmeester)

&nbsp;

## 1. 📝 SUMMARY

**[Quick Start](#2--quick-start)** · [Overview](#overview) · [How It Works](#4--how-it-works) · [Workflows](#5--everyday-workflows) · [Skills](#6--skills) · [Hooks](#7--hooks-plugins-and-extensions) · [Agents](#8--agents) · [Commands](#9--commands) · [Runtimes](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) · [Doctor](#11--doctor-commands) · [FAQ](#15--faq)

---

An assistant framework that gives your AI coding agent a memory, a paper trail and a team of specialists.

All of it lives inside your own repository, as files you can read and diff.

Built for Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi Agent, Devin, Cursor CLI, DeepSeek Harness and Mistral Vibe.

**What's inside**

- **Gate System** - six gates check every request before the agent acts: prior context, the right skill, a spec folder and the repository rules
- **Skill Advisor** - routes every prompt to the right skill and workflow using 5-lane fusion and a live skill graph, so you never need to remember a command
- **Spec Kit Framework** - spec folders with plans, tasks and a 42-rule validator, grouped into tracks, sub-tracks and phases, with progress saved into each folder and found again by keyword search after any reset or compaction
- **Session Handover** - endless sessions: near the context limit, the work moves to a fresh session in the same terminal, which keeps the goal, the session name, the running command, the launch flags and, except on Vibe, the model
- **Autonomous Deep Loops** - research and review loops that run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says done
- **CLI Orchestration** - hand work to another AI CLI on its own subscription, with any provider and model you have set up
- **14 On-Demand Skills** - seven parent hubs with 42 modes, plus standalone skills for specs, git, prompts and Orca
- **26 Shared Hooks** - session handover, goals, routing, quality checks, git and secret guards, running in all eight runtimes through one thin adapter each
- **11 Specialized Agents** - focused roles for implementation, review, research, docs, design and orchestration
- **17 Doctor Commands** - install Skilled in your own project, check the setup and keep it current
- **Code Mode MCP** - external MCP tools called through one TypeScript interface

&nbsp;

## 2. 🚀 QUICK START

### Install

You need Node.js, npm, git and a POSIX shell. Node 24.22.1 or a newer 24.x is the one line every part accepts. Code Mode runs only on Node 24. The rest also runs on 22.23.4 and 26.12.1 or newer releases on those lines.

The [install guide](INSTALL-SKILLED.md#2-install-a-skilled-checkout) walks through every step. In short, clone the repository and build the Spec Kit and the Skill Advisor:

```bash
git clone https://github.com/MichelKerkmeester/skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops.git
cd skilled-agent-harness_spec-driven-loops

npm --prefix .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit install
npm --prefix .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit run build

npm --prefix .skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime install
npm --prefix .skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime run build
node .skilled/bin/skill-advisor.cjs list-tools --format json
```

The Spec Kit comes first, because the session hooks load its build and the advisor build uses its compiler.
[0m$ [0msed -n '225,300p' README.md
### Handing Over A Long Session

A long session can hand its work to a fresh one before the context window fills. Near the limit, the session writes a handoff note and saves its spec folder. A small wrapper then starts the same runtime in the same terminal, and the new session claims the note. The running command, the goal and the session name carry over, and so does the model on every runtime except Vibe.

Handover is on by default for Claude Code. The [Session Handover](#session-handover) hook below shows how to turn it on for the others.

&nbsp;

## 5. 🔄 EVERYDAY WORKFLOWS

### You Just Ask

You never need to remember a command. Describe the task in plain words and the session handles the rest. On every prompt, the [skill advisor](#skill-advisor) reads the request and tells the agent which skill fits. When a workflow fits too, the brief names its command and how to run it: start it now, start it with its setup questions or offer it to you first.

The session automation covers the rest of the path. The [spec gate](#spec-gate) asks once which spec folder the work belongs to. The [session lifecycle](#session-lifecycle) hooks prime the context at start and save continuity at stop and compaction. Near the context limit, [session handover](#session-handover) carries the work into a fresh session.

The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open while a P0 is active or a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

### Create And Update Skills, Agents And Commands

Extending Skilled follows the same framework that built it. The [sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md) hub holds a template, a set of standards and a validator for each kind of component. Ask for a new skill, agent or command in plain words and the advisor loads the matching sk-doc mode, so the result matches the rest of the repository.

Each kind also has a `/create` command for when you want the workflow directly. `/create:sk-skill` creates or updates a skill, and `/create:skill-parent` scaffolds a parent hub with its modes. `/create:agent` and `/create:command` cover agents and slash commands. `/create:readme`, `/create:changelog`, `/create:goal` and `/create:repo-rule` cover the documents around them. Every one runs with `:auto` or with `:confirm` to pause at each step.

Updating works the same way as creating. Name the component and the change, and the workflow edits it in place, checks it against its template and runs the sk-doc validator. A new skill needs no registration step, because the advisor finds it from its own metadata.

&nbsp;

## 6. 🧰 SKILLS

Fourteen skills ship with Skilled. Gate 2 loads one when the advisor matches it to a task. Seven of them are parent hubs. A hub holds several modes and routes each request to the mode that fits, 42 modes in all. The other seven skills work on their own.

### Parent Hubs

**[sk-code](.skilled/skills/sk-code/README.md)** writes, reviews and verifies code in the stack you are in. Its five modes are quality, for the checks after implementation, and review, for findings-first code review. CI covers GitHub Actions workflows and frontend covers CSS, HTML and browser work. The skilled mode holds the standards for this repository's own TypeScript, Python, shell and config code.

**[sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md)** authors documentation and components. Its fifteen modes create skills, parent hubs, READMEs, agents, commands, feature catalogs, manual testing playbooks, benchmarks, changelogs, diffs, frontmatter, repo rules and goals. Two more run quality control and apply the human voice rules.

**[sk-design](.skilled/skills/sk-design/SKILL.md)** turns one design identity into an artifact. Its three modes extract a measured style reference from a live site, draw a diagram and build a chart.

**[system-deep-loop](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md)** runs the autonomous loops over one shared runtime. Its two modes are deep research and deep review.

**[cli-external-orchestration](.skilled/skills/cli-external-orchestration/README.md)** hands work to another AI CLI. Its eight modes are bridges to OpenCode, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Devin, Pi, DeepSeek Harness and Mistral Vibe.

**[cli-classifier](.skilled/skills/cli-classifier/README.md)** asks a hosted classifier for typed judgments. It has one mode today, the Jev transport, and future classifiers join as new modes.

**[mcp-tooling](.skilled/skills/mcp-tooling/README.md)** bridges outside tools through Code Mode. Its eight modes cover Chrome DevTools, ClickUp, the Aside browser, Notion and Obsidian. Three more are read-only design sources: Figma, Refero and Mobbin.

### Standalone Skills

**[system-spec-kit](.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/README.md)** owns spec folders, documentation levels, the validator, continuity and lexical search. Every file change goes through it.

**[system-skill-advisor](.skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/README.md)** routes each prompt to the skill that fits, through a daemon-backed CLI.

---HEAD---
---
title: "v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover"
description: "Claude Code hands over by default, vendored installs get stricter overlay checks and search says what it left out. Skilled also needs a patched Node.js and keeps .env values away from agents on all eight runtimes."
trigger_phrases:
  - "v4.1.0.0 release notes"
  - "skilled v4.1.0.0"
  - "dotenv secret guard"
  - "patched node releases required"
  - "goal carried across a hop"
  - "strict overlay writes"
  - "hub skill names its command"
  - "lookup reports total candidates"
  - "mistral vibe runtime hooks"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
---
# v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover

Long sessions now hand over cleanly, and Claude Code does it by default.

- Near its context limit, a session writes a handoff note and a fresh one picks up in the same terminal.
- The hop carries the running command, the goal and the session's name.
- Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe now finish a hop too.

The skill advisor and the deep loops now say more and guess less.

- The advisor names the slash command behind a hub skill.
- It says whether the agent may run that command, start it or should ask first.
- Mistral Vibe joins as the eighth runtime, with Skilled's guards and advisor brief in every session.
- A deep review fails while a required check still fails, and a script makes that call.

Search, the spec commands and the overlay checks now report what really happened.

- Search answers say how much they left out.
- The spec commands save what they report and check their own work.
- Overlay checks in a vendored project refuse a bad write before it lands.

This release also changes what you install and what an agent may read.

- Skilled now needs a patched Node.js release.
- A new guard keeps the values in a `.env` file away from every agent, on all eight runtimes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> Six changes can break an existing setup. Read these before you update.
>
> 1. Old session relaunch switch names are no longer read. [Old switch names](#c-old-switch-names-stop-working)
> 2. An open handoff note in the old state folder is not found. [Moving from session relaunch](#c-moving-from-session-relaunch)
> 3. The history lookup is no longer offered after a compaction. [Claimed note history](#c-a-claimed-note-offers-the-senders-history)
> 4. `/deep:ai-council` is gone, so run `/speckit:ai-council`. [AI Council](#c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent)
> 5. The standalone git pre-commit hook is gone. [Hook folders](#c-folders-say-what-they-guard)
> 6. Code Mode needs Node 24.22.1 or newer on 24. [Node.js](#c-patched-releases-are-required)

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

Several parts of Skilled said one thing and did another. Handover existed, but most people never had it on. Checks could pass while something underneath had failed. This release closes those gaps.

- Handover shipped off, so most Claude Code users never had it.
- A hop mid-command came back as a plain chat.

=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.0.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0, completed runs can be extended"
description: "Completed deep-research and deep-review runs take --extend=N, which adds iterations in the same session and keeps the prior synthesis or report as a snapshot."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.0.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.0.0"
  - "completed run extension"
  - "run_extended ledger event"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.0.0
---
# v2.1.0.0, Completed Runs Can Be Extended

A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to get more iterations. `--extend=N` reopens the finished run in place, so the same session and generation carry on and the earlier results stay on record.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Completed runs take `--extend=N`.** `/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` add N iterations to a completed run in the same session and generation.
- **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** A run that is not complete, a fan-out config, or a value that is not a positive integer stops with a named error.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Without `--extend`, nothing changes.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/001-completed-run-extension`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.1.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0, source diversity reads derived source classes"
description: "Research SOURCE nodes get a quality_class derived from their path when they are stored, so source diversity scores real classes. A question answered only from code still blocks."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.1.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.1.0"
  - "source diversity quality class"
  - "derived quality_class"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.1.0
---
# v2.1.1.0, Source Diversity Reads Derived Source Classes

Source diversity could not score real classes, because no producer set `quality_class`. Research SOURCE nodes now get a class when they are stored, so the diversity signal reads what the sources are.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Research sources are classified when stored.** `upsert.cjs` sets `quality_class` on each research SOURCE node from its path first, then its label, then its id. A value the leaf supplies is overwritten.
- **Source diversity has real values on normal runs.** The signal counts the distinct classes a question's findings cite, instead of moving only on fixtures that typed a class in.
- **A code-only question still blocks.** A question answered only from code files scores 1.0 and stops at `source_diversity_guard`, by design. Code plus its tests scores 2.0 and passes.
- **The threshold is unchanged.** The 1.5 threshold and the averaging rule did not change.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/007-source-diversity-quality-class`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.2.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0, merged fan-out reviews keep their coverage"
description: "A merged two-lineage review fan-out keeps the coverage its lineages recorded: dimension, traceability, search, candidate, divergence and ruled-out surfaces are carried through the merge."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.2.0"
  - "fan-out review coverage merge"
  - "divergenceByLineage"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.2.0
---
# v2.1.2.0, Merged Fan-Out Reviews Keep Their Coverage

A review that fans out to several lineages used to lose the coverage each lineage had recorded when the results were merged. The merge now carries those surfaces through, so the merged review shows what every lineage covered.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Dimension coverage is a union.** `dimensionCoverage` holds the dimensions every lineage covered, with sorted keys.
- **Traceability keeps the strictest status.** Per protocol, `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. An undecided status never replaces a decided one.
- **Search and candidate coverage are merged.** `searchCoverage` holds sorted unions. Each `candidateCoverage` bucket lists the lineages that reported it.
- **Divergence stays per lineage.** `divergenceByLineage` keeps each lineage's pivot state under its label. No merged `divergence` is written, because a merged current focus would be invented.
- **Ruled-out candidates name their lineage.** Each entry carries a `lineage` field and is deduplicated on lineage and id.
- **The merge is order-independent.** Lineages are read in label order, so the same lineages give the same merged result whatever order they arrive in.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. This release adds the data only. No stop decision reads these surfaces yet.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/009-fanout-review-coverage-merge`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.3.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.3.0, free text no longer steers Next Focus"
description: "Research and review Next Focus follow open questions and the leaf's recommendation, not status sentences or answered questions. Normal runs change with no flag."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.3.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.3.0"
  - "next focus free text"
  - "carried-forward open questions"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.3.0
---

# v2.1.3.0, Free Text No Longer Steers Next Focus

Next Focus could follow a sentence that only reported progress. A run then spent a round on a settled question. Next Focus now follows open questions and the latest leaf's recommendation.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research carries forward only open questions. Status sentences and answered questions no longer return.
- Research reads the latest leaf's `Recommended Next Focus`. It leads while a machine question is open, unless that focus is already answered or rejected.
- Review names the first uncovered dimension. Leaf free text that names no dimension is ignored.
- Imperative items, such as `Investigate the review twin`, no longer carry forward. The leaf's recommendation can still carry one into Next Focus.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases failed before the change and pass after it. The three reducer and continuity suites pass, and every existing case is kept.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Normal research and review runs use the new focus rules with no flag.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/004-focus-hijack-fix`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.4.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0, divergent pivots get one quorum retry"
description: "A divergent pivot gets three council seats per attempt and one retry when quorum misses. A run now uses at most 18 seat outputs."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.4.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.4.0"
  - "divergent pivot quorum retry"
  - "maxCouncilSeatOutputs"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.4.0
---

# v2.1.4.0, Divergent Pivots Get One Quorum Retry

A divergent pivot ended on its first quorum miss. One missed seat could end the pivot. A pivot now gets one retry before it stops.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` now means three per council attempt.
- A first-attempt quorum miss gets one retry.
- A second miss still ends the pivot. No third attempt opens.
- A later pivot is no longer refused because earlier pivots used seats.
- A run uses at most 18 seat outputs. That is three pivots with six seats each.
- Research and review pivots follow the same rule.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Eleven new cases cover the seat budget, the retry and the attempt identity. The divergent pivot suites pass. Attempt 1 keeps its existing paths and event ids.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Pivots written before this change still load. A first-attempt quorum miss now runs one more council round.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/012-pivot-hygiene`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.5.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0, landed iterations are not dispatched twice"
description: "A research or review iteration that already landed is no longer dispatched again after three verify failures. Every other failure still gets one redispatch."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.5.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.5.0"
  - "landed iteration redispatch"
  - "executor_missing redispatch"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.5.0
---

# v2.1.5.0, Landed Iterations Are Not Dispatched Twice

A research or review iteration could be dispatched again after it had landed. The second worker would find its files written. It would then refuse to overwrite them. The loop waited on a run that could not succeed.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Three verify failures skip the redispatch. They are `executor_missing`, `findings_not_enumerated` and `ledger_backing_missing`.
- Those failures appear only after the iteration's narrative, state record and delta exist.
- Research and review follow the same rule.
- Every other failure still gets one redispatch, including route proof.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases cover the landed-iteration skip across the verify, counter and reducer suites. Those suites pass. The existing append-only redispatch case still passes.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Expect one dispatch fewer for these three failures.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.6.0.md ===
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.6.0, research runs use the configured Claude config dir"
description: "A deep-research run on cli-claude-code runs Claude under the config dir the operator configured. Its audit record names that dir."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.6.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.6.0"
  - "claude-config-dir research"
  - "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR research"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.6.0
---

# v2.1.6.0, Research Runs Use the Configured Claude Config Dir

A research run on Claude ignored the config dir the operator set. It ran under whichever account the shell had. Research now honors the configured dir.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research accepts `--claude-config-dir` on `cli-claude-code` runs.
- The dir is checked before the prompt is read. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused.
- Claude runs under the configured dir. It overrides an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- The audit record names the dir the run used.
- A run that names no dir behaves as before.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

New cases cover the flag, its validation and the audit record. All ten dispatch cases pass in a detached run. Inside a Claude session, two skip under the Claude ancestry guard.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Set `configDir` in the run's executor config to pin the account. An inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` still reaches a research run that names no dir. Stripping it would change every default run, so it stays as it is.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/002-research-claude-config-dir`

title: "deep-review v1.11.18.0, a review run holds below its minimum iterations"
