# v1.11.18.0, A Review Run Holds Below Its Minimum Iterations

The minimum-iteration floor applied to research runs only. A review that reached a stop candidate before its configured floor could stop early, so the floor now applies to review runs too.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **A review below its floor continues.** A stop candidate below `minIterations` returns `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached`, and the run releases once the floor is reached.
- **The floor is checked before the legal-stop gates.** The review workflow's stop order names it as item `4a`, and the deep-review README says what it does.
- **The shipped floor is unchanged.** The shipped floor of 2 and its stop-path replay are the same, with fixtures untouched.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. A review that sets `minIterations` above its previous stop now runs until the floor is reached.

&nbsp;

# v1.12.0.0, Custom Review Dimensions Are Supported

A review can now name its own dimensions, such as performance. Before this, a custom name was never marked covered. So the review could never reach its clean stop. Setup now accepts custom names. The loop tracks and covers them, and the review prompt lists them. Runs with only the four canonical dimensions are unchanged.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Custom dimensions such as performance now reach the clean stop.
- Setup accepts custom names after the four canonical ones.
- Custom dimensions keep their configured order after the canonical four.
- A finding can name a custom dimension.
- Canonical-only runs keep the same registry, dashboard and verdict.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. To review a custom dimension, add its name to `review_dimensions`, such as `correctness,performance`.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/008-review-dimension-coverage`
=== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.13.0.0.md ===
---
title: "deep-review v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review"
description: "Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. An opt-in expansive depth mode admits, steers toward and closes them. Runs without the flag keep their output."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.13.0.0"
  - "deep-review 1.13.0.0"
  - "expansive depth mode"
  - "review frontier"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.13.0.0
---

# v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review

Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. This release adds an opt-in expansive depth mode for that.

- Reviews propose sub-targets while they work.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the frontier.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A review that checks its target in one pass can look finished too early. Nothing could split a sub-target out once the review began. So a large target could stop before each part got its own look.

- A narrow pass looked the same as a thorough one.
- The new mode lets the review widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a review with one flag](#c-widen-a-review-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Sub-targets exist before the first pass](#c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets) | The review splits its target before iteration one. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Pruned proposals name their reason](#c-pruned-proposals-name-their-reason) | A pruned proposal records the check or the rule that stopped it. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Each sub-target needs every dimension](#c-each-sub-target-needs-every-dimension) | A sub-target counts as answered only after all its dimensions. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Open sub-targets hold a clean stop](#c-open-sub-targets-hold-a-clean-stop) | A review that looks clean keeps going while a sub-target stays open. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Budget splits across three depth bands](#c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths. | Steering and Stopping |
| [The pivot waits for a closed frontier](#c-the-pivot-waits-for-a-closed-frontier) | The divergent pivot runs only after the frontier closes. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Lineages share the sub-targets](#c-lineages-share-the-sub-targets) | A fan-out review deals its sub-targets across its lineages. | Fan-Out Runs |
| [The merge names a missing sub-target](#c-the-merge-names-a-missing-sub-target) | The merge warns when a dealt sub-target reached no lineage. | Fan-Out Runs |

&nbsp;

## Turning It On

Turning the mode on takes one flag. Without the flag, a review keeps its old behavior, and its dashboard gains no section.

#### Widen a review with one flag <a id="c-widen-a-review-with-one-flag"></a>

- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a `/deep:review` run.
- Leave the flag out, or pass `--depth-mode=default`, to keep the old behavior.
- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md` finds the flag row.

#### Default runs keep their output <a id="c-default-runs-keep-their-output"></a>

- A default run writes the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack and stop config as before.
- The mode key appears only on expansive runs.
- CLI lineage prompts gain one paragraph in the agent persona.
- That paragraph has no effect on a default run.
- Its waiver awaits operator sign-off.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive review adds a `## 10B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size, pruned sub-targets, revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

&nbsp;

## Admitting Sub-Targets

A review splits its target into sub-targets before it starts. Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the frontier.

#### Sub-targets exist before the first pass <a id="c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets"></a>

- The review splits its target into depth-one sub-targets before iteration one.
- Each sub-target gets a scope and a question.
- A repeated launch adds no second set of sub-targets.
- A review writes its sub-targets to its own store and never to an inbox.

#### Every proposal passes six fixed checks <a id="c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks"></a>

- The checks run in one fixed order.
- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
- Relevance needs evidence, a scope inside the review target and a parent that exists.
- Dedupe refuses a question the review already holds.
- Depth stops at three levels.
- Fan-out caps each parent at six children and caps the total count.
- A child cannot hold more budget than its parent.
- A cycle is refused.

#### Pruned proposals name their reason <a id="c-pruned-proposals-name-their-reason"></a>

- The first failing check decides the reason.
- A sub-target that arrives with a reason is settled at seed time.
- Two seed reasons exist: `merge-no-own-diff` for a merge with no diff of its own, and `generated-artifact` for a generated index rebuild.
- A pruned proposal keeps its reason in the store.
- Only an admitted proposal joins the frontier.

&nbsp;

## Steering and Stopping

Once sub-targets exist, the review works the active node's next uncovered dimension. It stops only when no sub-target stays open.

#### Each sub-target needs every dimension <a id="c-each-sub-target-needs-every-dimension"></a>

- A sub-target answers once every configured dimension has evidence behind it.
- The active sub-target's next uncovered dimension steers the next iteration.
- Next Focus picks the blocked-stop recovery first, then the pivot, then the active sub-target.
- The leaf's own focus comes after that, and the first uncovered dimension comes last.

#### Open sub-targets hold a clean stop <a id="c-open-sub-targets-hold-a-clean-stop"></a>

- While a sub-target stays open, a review stop candidate becomes `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`.
- The hold covers the clean stop, which is a hard stop, as well as the composite stop.
- The iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, the off setting, the max-iterations policy and the review floor still decide first.
- Stuck recovery closes the active sub-target as `exhausted`, so a sub-target the review cannot fund stops holding the review open.

#### Budget splits across three depth bands <a id="c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands"></a>

- The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths one, two and three.
- Unused budget rolls down only after its band has no open sub-target, and it never rolls up.
- A band that spends its share closes its open sub-targets as `exhausted` with reason `budget`.

#### The pivot waits for a closed frontier <a id="c-the-pivot-waits-for-a-closed-frontier"></a>

- The divergent review pivot refuses to run while a sub-target stays open.
- It seeds one revisit direction for each sub-target the frontier closed as `exhausted` or `blocked`.
- A default run keeps its pivot unchanged.

&nbsp;

## Fan-Out Runs

A fan-out review deals its depth-one sub-targets across its lineages. The merge then checks the split.

#### Lineages share the sub-targets <a id="c-lineages-share-the-sub-targets"></a>

- Open depth-one sub-targets deal round-robin across the lineages, in node-id order.
- Each lineage writes its share to its own store before it spawns.
- A review lineage has no inbox.
- The orchestration summary records which lineage holds which sub-target.

#### The merge names a missing sub-target <a id="c-the-merge-names-a-missing-sub-target"></a>

- The merge checks that every dealt sub-target reached a lineage.
- A sub-target that no lineage reports gets a warning on stderr, named `frontier_partition_guard_failed`.
- The merged output carries `frontier_partition_guard` with the missing identifiers.
- The merge still completes.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

The default path was checked against a baseline. The baseline was captured before any review depth-mode code existed. The suites below ran on this branch.

#### Before and After <a id="c-before-and-after"></a>

| Check | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Count of `--depth-mode` in the review command reference | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the flag row |
| Count of `"depthMode"` in the review config | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the `default` value |
| Golden default-path suite | Not present at the baseline commit | 14 of 14 cases pass |

<details>
<summary><b>What it costs</b></summary>

- An expansive review runs one admission step on each iteration.
- The cost was not measured.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>Tests and scenarios</b></summary>

- Golden default path: 14 passed, 0 failed.
- Review admission helper: 18 passed, 0 failed.
- Shared admission table: 24 passed, 0 failed.
- Reducer: 32 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision: 92 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision YAML contract: 34 passed, 0 failed.
- Divergent pivot: 26 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out runner: 207 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out merge: 88 passed, 0 failed.
- Review playbook validator: PASS, with the seven new scenarios.
- Not yet run: the live pilot on a real review target. It runs on the integration tip after merge.

</details>

&nbsp;

## Upgrade Notes

No action is needed for existing reviews. Only a run that passes the flag changes.

#### Before You Start <a id="c-before-you-start"></a>

1. Keep your review commands as they are. They behave as before.

<details>
<summary><b>Turning It On</b></summary>

- Add `--depth-mode=expansive` to a review run to let it add sub-targets.
- Read the `## 10B. FRONTIER` section of the dashboard.

</details>

&nbsp;

# v1.15.16.0, The Prompt Pack Names research.md and the Next-Focus Heading

The research prompt pack now agrees with the research agent definition on two points. A fan-out lineage can write its own synthesis, and the reducer reads the next focus the pack asks for.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **The pack allows `research/research.md`.** ALLOWED WRITE PATHS lists `research.md` beside the iterations folder, and only when `progressiveSynthesis` is `true`.
- **The narrative heading is `Recommended Next Focus`.** The heading list ends in the heading the research reducer parses, so the next focus is read from the pack's own wording.
- **A lockstep test guards both points.** It fails when the pack and the agent definition drift apart on either one.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. The agent definition and the runtime mirrors are unchanged.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/005-prompt-pack-contract-alignment`

title: "deep-research v1.15.17.0, focus and findings checks read the leaf fields"

# v1.15.17.0, Focus and Findings Checks Read the Leaf Fields

Two checks could not see the fields a leaf wrote. Both now read them. They run on every research run, with no flag.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- The state log keeps ten leaf fields.
- The list includes `findingsCount`, `keyQuestions` and `sourcesQueried`.
- The `findings_not_enumerated` gate reads the real `findingsCount`.
- `focusAlignment` is live on default research runs.
- It judges the latest record that carries both question lists.
- Reducer consumers now receive the fields.
- A single `keyQuestions` entry resolves its question.
- `sourcesQueried` feeds the source list.
- The dashboard shows `focusTrack` and `findingsCount`.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

The replay ran five real generation-two deltas. `focusAlignment` failed on four of them. The findings gate and route proof passed on all five. Iterations 2, 3 and 5 answered an unlisted question. Iteration 4 wrote its key question as a bare id, `Q6`. That id does not match the answered text.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

A `focusAlignment` failure can now hold a run at CONTINUE. The hold clears on the next aligned iteration. The guard judges only the latest record with both lists. The prompt pack asks for the exact question text. Write `keyQuestions` with no id prefix, such as `Q1:`.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/006-state-log-leaf-fields`

# v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research

Deep research stops once its starting questions are answered. This release adds an opt-in mode that lets a run grow its own questions.

- Leaves propose new sub-questions while the run works.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the question set.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A run that answers its starting questions narrowly looks finished. Nothing could add a question once the run began. So a broad topic could stop early.

- Narrow coverage looked the same as converged coverage.
- The new mode lets the run widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a run with one flag](#c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Open questions exist before the first pass](#c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass) | The run has open sub-questions before iteration one. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Rejected proposals name their reason](#c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason) | A pruned proposal records the check that stopped it. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Focus goes to the shallowest open node](#c-focus-goes-to-the-shallowest-open-node) | The next iteration works the least deep open question. | Steering and Stopping |
| [A run keeps going while nodes stay open](#c-a-run-keeps-going-while-nodes-stay-open) | A run that looks finished continues while a node is open. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Budget is shared across three depth bands](#c-budget-is-shared-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits across depths. Unused budget rolls down. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Finished nodes stop counting as open](#c-finished-nodes-stop-counting-as-open) | A finished node resolves its question and records why it ended. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Lineages share the starting nodes](#c-lineages-share-the-starting-nodes) | A fan-out run deals its starting nodes across its lineages. | Fan-Out Runs |
| [The merge names a missing node](#c-the-merge-names-a-missing-node) | The merge warns when a starting node reached no lineage. | Fan-Out Runs |

&nbsp;

## Turning It On

Turning the mode on takes one flag. Without the flag, a run keeps its old behavior. Its dashboard gains no new section.

#### Widen a run with one flag <a id="c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag"></a>

- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a research run.
- Leave the flag out to keep the old behavior.
- Passing `--depth-mode=default` also keeps the old behavior.
- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md` finds the flag row.

#### Default runs keep their output <a id="c-default-runs-keep-their-output"></a>

- A default run writes the same registry, dashboard, focus and prompt pack as before.
- The stop config echo stays the same.
- The mode key appears only on expansive runs.
- CLI lineage prompts gain one paragraph in the agent persona.
- That paragraph has no effect on a default run.
- Its waiver awaits operator sign-off.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive run adds a `## 6B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size and pruned nodes.
- It also shows revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

&nbsp;

## Admitting New Questions

Leaves propose sub-questions as rows. The run checks each one before it changes the question set.

#### Open questions exist before the first pass <a id="c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass"></a>

- The run splits its topic into starting nodes before iteration one.
- The model running the loop writes those nodes.
- It works from the topic and its key questions.
- Every starting node passes the same checks.
- A repeated launch adds no second set of nodes.

#### Every proposal passes six fixed checks <a id="c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks"></a>

- The checks run in one fixed order.
- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
- Relevance needs evidence, a scope and a parent that exists.
- Dedupe refuses a question the run already holds.
- Depth stops at three levels.
- Fan-out caps each parent at six children.
- Fan-out also caps the total node count.
- A child cannot hold more budget than its parent.
- A cycle is refused.

#### Rejected proposals name their reason <a id="c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason"></a>

- The first failing check decides the reason.
- A pruned proposal keeps its reason in the node store.
- A pruned proposal never reaches the research inbox.
- An admitted proposal adds one question with origin `frontier`.

&nbsp;

## Steering and Stopping

Once nodes exist, the run works the shallowest one. It stops only when no node stays open within budget.

#### Focus goes to the shallowest open node <a id="c-focus-goes-to-the-shallowest-open-node"></a>

- Next Focus picks the open node at the lowest depth.
- Ties go to a node with no covering finding.
- The oldest node wins any remaining tie.
- The focus line reads `Frontier node <id> (depth <n>, scope <scope>): <question>`.
- A visit to a node does not answer its question.
- A reworded focus still counts as a visit.
- It must keep the node's marker.

#### A run keeps going while nodes stay open <a id="c-a-run-keeps-going-while-nodes-stay-open"></a>

- A run that looks converged keeps going while a node is open within budget.
- The veto applies only when the expansive flag is on.
- The iteration cap still wins.
- Consecutive failures, stuck recovery and pause still win.
- The off setting and the max-iterations policy still win.
- A held run reports `frontierOpen` as its reason detail.

#### Budget is shared across three depth bands <a id="c-budget-is-shared-across-three-depth-bands"></a>

- The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across three depth bands.
- Unused budget rolls down to a deeper band.
- Unused budget never rolls up.
- A band that runs out closes its open nodes as exhausted for budget.

#### Finished nodes stop counting as open <a id="c-finished-nodes-stop-counting-as-open"></a>

- A node that finishes resolves its question and records its status.
- A finished node stops lowering question coverage and source diversity.
- A stuck run closes its active node as exhausted.
- Focus then moves on.
- A run whose nodes have all finished can reach a clean stop.

&nbsp;

## Fan-Out Runs

Fan-out runs share the starting nodes across their lineages. Then the merge checks the split.

#### Lineages share the starting nodes <a id="c-lineages-share-the-starting-nodes"></a>

- The starting nodes deal out round-robin across the lineages.
- Each lineage writes its share to its own store first.
- The orchestration summary records which lineage holds which node.
- The merged result carries the frontier from every lineage.

#### The merge names a missing node <a id="c-the-merge-names-a-missing-node"></a>

- The merge checks that every starting node reached a lineage.
- A node that no lineage holds is named in a warning on stderr.
- The merge still finishes.
- One missing node does not cost the synthesis.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

The default path was checked against a baseline. The baseline was captured before any depth-mode code existed. The suites below ran on this branch.

#### Before and After <a id="c-before-and-after"></a>

| Check | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `git grep -c -- '--depth-mode'` on the research command reference | No match, exit 1 | One match, the flag row |
| `git grep -c -- '"depthMode"'` on the research config | No match, exit 1 | One match, the `default` value |

<details>
<summary><b>What it costs</b></summary>

- An expansive run calls the admission helper on each iteration.
- That adds process calls for admission, the frontier block and the graph mirror.
- The cost was not measured.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>Tests and scenarios</b></summary>

- Golden default path: 12 passed, 0 failed.
- Admission helper: 24 passed, 0 failed.
- Reducer: 49 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision: 79 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision YAML contract: 22 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out runner: 203 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out merge: 84 passed, 0 failed.
- Not yet run: a live research run with `--depth-mode=expansive` on a real topic.

</details>

&nbsp;

## Upgrade Notes

No action is needed for existing runs. Only a run that passes the flag changes.

#### Before You Start <a id="c-before-you-start"></a>

1. Keep your research commands as they are. They behave as before.

<details>
<summary><b>Turning It On</b></summary>

- Add `--depth-mode=expansive` to a research run to let it widen.
- Read the `## 6B. FRONTIER` section of the dashboard.
- The section shows what the run added.

</details>

# deep-research

> Investigate a broad question over many rounds, keep every finding on disk and stop when new evidence runs out.

The run leaves a packet on disk, so you can resume it later or audit how each finding arrived.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What you get |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | A broad question that needs several rounds, where each round builds on the last |
| **Invoke with** | `/deep:research:auto "topic"` or `/deep:research:confirm "topic"` or prose that names autoresearch |
| **Works on** | Web sources and repo files, read through the LEAF agent's tools |
| **Produces** | `research/research.md`, an iteration trail, a findings registry and a convergence dashboard |

### What It Does

`deep-research` runs one question through repeated rounds until the evidence stops growing. Every round starts a fresh `@deep-research` agent that reads the packet from disk, investigates one focus and writes its findings back. The workflow then reduces the state, scores novelty against the accumulated record and decides whether to continue. Start an unattended run with `/deep:research:auto "topic"`. Use `/deep:research:confirm "topic"` when you want an approval point before the first round and before synthesis.

It does not run a single-pass codebase lookup (`@context` does that) or audit code (`deep-review` does that).

### Why This Skill Exists

A long investigation inside one conversation loses detail as findings fill the context window. Pruning to keep the window small removes the early evidence. Reading prior results again costs the next round its working room. This skill writes every finding to disk, starts each round clean and stops on a measure of new information instead of a hunch.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Auto resolves setup from the arguments and runs without approval prompts. Confirm asks for approval at setup and after each round. It also holds synthesis behind a gate.

```bash
/deep:research:auto "WebSocket reconnection strategies across browsers"
/deep:research:confirm "Distributed cache invalidation patterns"
```

Expected output: auto resolves setup without a prompt, writes the first packet files under `{spec_folder}/research/` and starts the loop. It asks one targeted question only when the spec folder is ambiguous. Confirm shows the consolidated setup prompt first and waits for your answers.

**Step 2: Run the primary workflow.** Bound the run with flags.

```bash
/deep:research:auto "API backpressure patterns" --max-iterations 6 --convergence 0.03
```

Expected output: the loop stops at the six-round cap or when the legal-stop gates pass, then writes `{spec_folder}/research/research.md` with the synthesized findings. The iteration trail sits under `{spec_folder}/research/iterations/`.

`--executor=<kind>` selects the executor kind. The native kind dispatches the agent. The CLI kinds dispatch each round through `dispatch-iteration.cjs`. `cli-mistral-vibe` runs only as a fan-out lineage, so its inline branch fails closed. `--dry-run` previews setup and focus selection, then halts before the first write.

**Step 3: Rebuild the derived files.**

```bash
node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs <spec-folder>
```

Expected output: a JSON summary that names the files it rewrote and reports the iteration and corruption counts. Exit 0 means the derived files are current. Exit 2 means the reducer refused the state, usually because the log holds corruption. The resource-map fields stay null unless you add `--emit-resource-map`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

The workflow runs four phases: initialization, iteration, synthesis and save. Initialization classifies the packet as fresh, resume, completed-session or invalid-state, then writes the config and the strategy. It seeds the state log with its first line through the append gateway. The iteration phase repeats until a stop is legal: read state, check the pause sentinel, dispatch one fresh agent, validate its outputs and run the reducer. Synthesis compiles the iteration trail into `research.md`. Save writes continuity through `generate-context.js`.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Start["/deep:research:auto or :confirm"] --> Init["Init writes the packet"]
  Init --> Read["Read state and check convergence"]
  Read --> Dispatch["Dispatch one fresh @deep-research round"]
  Dispatch --> Reduce["Reducer rewrites derived state"]
  Reduce --> Stop{"Stop legal?"}
  Stop -- No --> Read
  Stop -- Yes --> Synth["Synthesis compiles research.md"]
  Synth --> Save["Save writes continuity"]
```

### The Packet Files

Every run keeps continuity under `{spec_folder}/research/`. A root spec writes there directly. A child-phase target writes flat on a first run and adds a `{basename(spec_folder)}-pt-{NN}` folder only when prior content exists for a different target.

| File | Owner | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `deep-research-config.json` | Workflow | Loop parameters and lineage. Read-only after init |
| `deep-research-state.jsonl` | Append gateway | Config record, iteration records and typed events |
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Reducer | Key questions, next focus, what worked and what failed |
| `findings-registry.json` | Reducer | Question state, key findings, ruled-out directions and blocked-stop history |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Reducer | Iteration table, trend, dead ends and active risks |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Agent | One round of cited findings |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | Agent | The structured delta for that round |
| `research.md` | Workflow | The canonical synthesis of the run |
| `resource-map.md` | Workflow | The optional evidence-derived map of cited files |
| `.deep-research-pause` | Operator | The pause sentinel checked before each dispatch |

The workflow owns the config and the synthesis. The agent writes its iteration file and delta, plus any progressive synthesis contribution the config allows. The reducer owns the strategy sections and the registry. It redraws the dashboard. Because every artifact is on disk, a crashed run resumes from the packet files.

### Convergence

A low novelty score only nominates STOP.

| Signal | Weight | Votes STOP when |
|---|---|---|
| Rolling average | 0.30 | The last three ratios average below `convergenceThreshold` |
| MAD noise floor | 0.35 | The latest ratio sits at or below `MAD * 1.4826` |
| Question entropy | 0.35 | Evidence-backed coverage reaches 0.85 |

A normalized score above 0.60 nominates STOP. The legal-stop gates then check that low novelty held for the required rounds, that every key question has an evidence-backed answer, that sources are diverse and that one weak source does not carry a finding. A missing graph decision blocks every non-terminal STOP. When a gate fails, the run records `blocked_stop` and continues with a recovery focus.

The shipped config floors convergence at 3 rounds, sets `stuckThreshold` to 3 and caps a run at 10 rounds and 120 minutes. Lower `--convergence` asks for more rounds. Raise it when the loop should finish sooner.

`--convergence-mode` changes the stop path. `off` disables convergence STOP while the hard caps stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window. `divergent` sends an eligible legal stop into one bounded pivot round instead of synthesis, then returns to the loop.

`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

### Stop Reasons

The workflow persists one `stopReason` value when the loop ends or pauses.

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `converged` | A legal stop passed and the run may exit |
| `maxIterationsReached` | The round cap was hit |
| `blockedStop` | A gate blocked a STOP candidate |
| `stuckRecovery` | Stuck detection triggered or recovery failed |
| `error` | An unrecoverable workflow or state error |
| `manualStop` | The operator asked the run to stop |
| `userPaused` | A pause sentinel halted the run |
| `maxDurationReached` | The wall-clock limit was hit |

### Fresh Context Per Iteration

Each dispatch starts a new agent with no memory of prior rounds. The agent reads the strategy and the state log, picks the next focus and returns after one round. Nothing carries over in context. Everything carries over in files. A crashed run therefore resumes from the packet files instead of starting over.

---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Run `deep-research` when a question needs several rounds and each round should shape the next. Run it when the work is unattended, such as an overnight investigation. Run it when you want the loop to stop on a measured signal instead of your judgment call.

Skip it for a single lookup, where a direct web search or the `@context` agent is faster. Skip it for implementation planning, where `/speckit:plan` owns the plan once context is in hand. Skip it for a code audit, where `deep-review` owns the pass.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `deep-review` | Audits the code a research run points at. The two share `runtime/` and keep separate convergence thresholds |
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec folder, its validation and the continuity save that closes a run |

`/speckit:plan` and `/speckit:implement` consume the report. `/speckit:resume` rebuilds context before you extend a completed run. `/speckit:save` writes a manual memory save, though a research run already saves at the end.

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The loop stops before the topic is covered | The threshold is loose for the breadth of the question | Lower `--convergence` to 0.03 or raise `--max-iterations` |
| The loop runs to the cap without converging | The weighted score stays under 0.60 or a legal-stop gate keeps blocking it | Read the dashboard for `BLOCKED STOPS` and the current focus, then narrow the focus or raise the threshold |
| The reducer exits 2 | The state log holds corruption and the reducer fails closed | Repair the bad line or rerun with `--lenient` to accept it |
| Derived files lag the last round | The reducer did not run after the write-back | Run the reducer command from QUICK START |
| A new run resumes an old packet | The config still holds an active lineage | Pass `--restart` or archive the packet and start again |
| The loop will not continue after a pause | `.deep-research-pause` is still in the packet | Remove `{spec_folder}/research/.deep-research-pause` and invoke the command again |
| Every round hits the tool-call cap | The focus is too wide for one round | Narrow `Next Focus` in `deep-research-strategy.md` to one sub-question |

---

## 6. FAQ

**Q: How does convergence decide to stop?**

A: A low novelty score only nominates STOP. The weighted score must clear 0.60 before a STOP is nominated. Then the legal-stop gates check novelty over the required rounds, question coverage, source diversity and graph evidence. A blocked gate writes a `blocked_stop` event and the loop continues with a recovery focus. The full decision order is in [the convergence reference](./references/convergence/convergence.md).

**Q: Why does each iteration start a fresh agent?**

A: A shared context window fills with stale findings. Later rounds read the same material through more clutter. A fresh LEAF agent per round plus state on disk means every round reads only what the packet holds. The agent never dispatches a sub-agent or nests another loop.

**Q: Where are the findings kept?**

A: Each round writes `{spec_folder}/research/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` and a delta file under `deltas/`. Synthesis compiles the trail into `research.md`. The reducer indexes the finding set in `findings-registry.json` and draws the trend in `deep-research-dashboard.md`.

**Q: What is the difference between resume and restart?**

A: `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and appends a `resumed` event. `restart` archives the current `research/` tree under `research_archive/`, mints a fresh `sessionId`, increments `generation` and appends a `restarted` event. `completed-continue` comes from `--extend=N`: it reopens a completed run in the same session, raises the cap to the runs so far plus N, snapshots the prior synthesis and appends an `extended` event.
`fork` is declared in the lifecycle vocabulary but not wired.

## 9. 💻 COMMANDS

Forty-four commands ship in six groups in the [commands folder](.skilled/commands/). Each one is a Markdown file, and most hand their steps to a YAML workflow beside it.

**Spec Kit (8).** `/speckit:complete`, `/speckit:plan`, `/speckit:implement`, `/speckit:resume`, `/speckit:save`, `/speckit:search`, `/speckit:handoff` and `/speckit:ai-council`. These run the spec workflow and its continuity, as [Everyday Workflows](#5--everyday-workflows) describes.

**Create (13).** `/create:sk-skill`, `/create:skill-parent`, `/create:agent`, `/create:command`, `/create:readme`, `/create:changelog`, `/create:feature-catalog`, `/create:manual-testing-playbook`, `/create:goal`, `/create:benchmark`, `/create:diff`, `/create:repo-rule` and `/create:with-human-voice`. These scaffold or revise one kind of artifact to the sk-doc standards.

**Deep (2).** `/deep:research` and `/deep:review` run the autonomous loops.

**Design (3).** `/design:extract` measures a live site into a style reference. `/design:chart` builds a standalone HTML chart, and `/design:diagram` draws an HTML and SVG diagram.

**Prompt (1).** `/prompt:improve` turns a rough prompt into a structured one.

**Doctor (17).** Health checks and repairs, listed under [Doctor Commands](#11--doctor-commands).

Four runtimes add a goal command of their own, listed under the [Goal](#goal) hook.

&nbsp;

# v1.9.9.0, Hand-Written Dashboards Refused

v1.9.9.0 stops a research lineage from merging when it skipped the reducer. A lineage whose dashboard lacks the reducer's stamp is refused. So a hand-written dashboard can no longer pass as reduced output.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **A hand-written dashboard no longer merges as reduced output.** A research lineage whose `deep-research-dashboard.md` lacks the reducer's stamp is refused. So its registry stays out of the merged result. The run lists it under `refused_reducer_provenance`. Only research lineages with an on-disk registry are checked. So review merges are unchanged.
- **The attribution table marks refused lineages.** Their verdict cell reads REFUSED. So the table cannot present a lineage as merged while the payload reports it refused.
- **A lineage with no dashboard still merges, with a warning.** Its provenance is unconfirmed rather than failed. The run lists it under `reducer_provenance_warnings`.
- **One bad dashboard refuses only its own lineage.** A dashboard that is a link or resolves outside the artifact root no longer aborts the merge. The other lineages merge as usual.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required.

&nbsp;

# =============================================================================
# .env.example  —  Environment variable template
# =============================================================================
#
# CONVENTIONS
#   - Every variable here is OPTIONAL except the Code Mode provider secrets in
#     Section 1. The system runs on sensible defaults when nothing is set.
#   - Non-secret lines are COMMENTED and show the DEFAULT value. Uncomment the
#     line and change the value to override that default.
#   - Flag semantics:
#       Graduated ON  = enabled by default; set  false  to DISABLE.
#       Opt-in OFF    = disabled by default; set  true   to ENABLE.
#   - A default shown as empty means "unset by default":  # VAR=    # ...
#   - Booleans accept  1/true/yes/on  (and their negatives) unless noted.
#
# WHERE THESE ARE READ
#   Code Mode loads a filled-in .env for its provider secrets (Section 1), and
#   nothing else in this repository loads it. Hooks, validators and git hooks
#   read the process environment. The validation off switches in Section 5, the
#   hook kill-switches in Section 8 and the Jev feature switches in Section 17
#   also read .skilled/hooks/hook-flags.env, where a saved switch stays on until
#   a set environment value overrides it. A switch written only in .env counts
#   where your shell or runtime exports it.
#
# SETTING ONE LOCALLY
#   This file is a template: no reader loads its values. Copy a line to where its
#   reader looks, uncomment it and change the value:
#     - export NAME=value in ~/.zshrc or ~/.bashrc works for every setting and wins
#       over hook-flags.env
#     - .skilled/hooks/hook-flags.env (copy hook-flags.env.example) for the hook,
#       validation and Jev switches, SESSION_HANDOVER_NOTIFY_HOPS and SESSION_HANDOVER_GOAL
#     - .claude/settings.local.json, env block, for Claude Code sessions only
#     - .env (copy this file) only for the Code Mode keys in Section 1
#   ENV_SECRET_GUARD_PEPPER (Section 8) is the exception: export it in your shell
#   profile only, never in any of the files above.
#   All four are gitignored. /doctor:env list shows every setting and where it is
#   set, by name only; /doctor:env NAME saves one after showing the exact line.
#
# EXHAUSTIVE REFERENCE
#   The full, source-generated SPECKIT_* feature-flag reference (types, read
#   sites, rollout notes) lives in:
#     .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/ENV-REFERENCE.md
#   This file is a practical template; that document is authoritative.
#
# =============================================================================

# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# 1. CODE MODE — MCP PROVIDER SECRETS  (fill these in)
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

# These are the ONLY required values. Leave blank to disable a provider.
# Code Mode resolves a manual's bare ${VAR} reference as {manual_name}_{VAR},
# and DOUBLES every underscore in the manual name: a manual called
# my_manual looking up ${API_KEY} needs my__manual_API_KEY.
# A key under any other spelling is simply not found, and the manual fails to
# register at server startup with no runtime symptom beyond a shorter tool list.
# Never commit a filled-in .env — keep secrets out of version control.

# GitHub  (github_GITHUB_TOKEN is an accepted alias)
github_GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=

# ClickUp needs no key here: the clickup_official manual signs in with OAuth on first launch

[0m$ [0msed -n '1,60p' .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/ENV-REFERENCE.md
---
title: SPECKIT Environment Variable Reference
description: Environment variables read by the surviving spec-kit engine (validation orchestrator, generated metadata, level contracts, continuity writer and runtime hook adapters), plus shared, advisor-owned and external run-surface names documented for adjacent operator setup.
---

# SPECKIT Environment Variable Reference

> Environment variables read by the spec-kit engine in this package, plus the shared embedding/IPC and skill-advisor variables that live outside it but are documented here because operators configure them alongside it.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

All variables are optional. The engine runs with sensible defaults when none are set. Variables use **graduated semantics** unless noted: they default to ON and you disable them by setting `=false`.

`/doctor:env` reads this file at run time, then adds every setting in the root `.env.example` that this file does not document, with the default and description written on that line. It shows a switch's default and where it is set, by name only, and saves a preference only after you approve the exact line and destination. The root README's Environment Settings section says where to set a value locally.

**What this package still is.** The memory engine was removed. The MCP transport (`context-server`), the memory tools and handlers, the memory database and its launcher, sqlite vector search, the in-package embedders, the evals and migrations, and the spec-memory CLI shim and plugin bridge are all gone. What remains is the spec-kit engine: the validation orchestrator, the graph and description metadata generators, the documentation level contracts, the continuity writer's imports, and the runtime hook adapters under `hooks/` for Claude, Codex, Cursor, Devin and Pi.

**What is documented elsewhere but kept here.** The skill advisor, the shared HF model server and its `hf-embed` socket, and the shared embeddings and IPC code under `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/shared` and `.skilled/bin` were not touched by the removal. Their variables keep rows below, and each row's Source column names the file that reads it so ownership is unambiguous. The DeepSeek Harness run surface is documented by name only for adjacent setup. Its runtime owns those settings.

**Flag convention:**

| Pattern | Meaning |
|---------|---------|
| `!== 'false'` | **Graduated ON**: enabled by default, set `false` to disable |
| `=== 'true'` | **Opt-in OFF**: disabled by default, set `true` to enable |
| `parseFloat(... \|\| 'N')` | Numeric with fallback default N |
| `?.trim()` | String, empty = use default |

### Flag inventory

**There is no longer a feature-flag registry to tabulate.** `lib/search/search-flags.ts` was deleted with the memory engine, taking about ninety retrieval, ranking, graph, cognitive and feedback gates with it. `lib/config/capability-flags.ts` survives but now declares six gates, all of them in [Section 3](#3-spec-validation-and-generated-metadata): `SPECKIT_GENERATOR_HARDENING`, `SPECKIT_GENERATED_METADATA_GRANDFATHER`, `SPECKIT_GENERATED_METADATA_DRIFT_GATE`, `SPECKIT_IDENTITY_MERGE_SAFETY`, `SPECKIT_IDEMPOTENT_DESCRIPTION_WRITES` and `SPECKIT_STATUS_COMPLETION_CONSISTENCY_GATE`.

Every variable below now lives in the section that owns its reader, and each row's Source column names that file. If you are looking for a flag that used to be in this table and cannot find it, it was retired: nothing reads it, and setting it does nothing.
Total unique variables documented: 214, counted as unique backticked names in the `Variable` column of every table below. Recount with that method when adding rows. Multi-variable cells count once per name. The flag tables name their variables in a `governing env var` column instead, so the four names that appear only there (`SPECKIT_DIRECTIVE_LIFECYCLE_DEDUP`, `SPECKIT_DIRECTIVE_LIFECYCLE_STATE_DIR`, `SPECKIT_ENTITY_CONFIG_PATH`, `SPECKIT_PI_DIRECTIVE_DEDUP`) sit outside this count. With them the document names 218. Every spec-kit-owned name must have a verified reader in its source. The DeepSeek Harness names are listed for adjacent setup and are verified against its external run-surface documentation.

### Data Quality and Generator Hardening (028/005)

Generator and validation-gate flags for the spec-data-quality and generator-hardening packets. Most have since graduated to default-on and enforcing. The two that stay off are the grandfather escape hatch and the status-completion gate, which is held in report mode until its existing backlog is reviewed. These are the live half of the flag surface: unlike the retrieval registry in the table above, changing one of these affects `validate.sh` and the metadata generators.

| flag name | default state (ON/OFF) | governing env var | which automation it gates | runtime read site |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Generator hardening | ON | `SPECKIT_GENERATOR_HARDENING` | Persists the graph-metadata `source_fingerprint`, routes the phase-parent classification and the derived children list through one `listPhaseChildren` enumeration, and moves access/freshness telemetry to the index-layer store so a read or resume no longer dirties the generated JSON | `lib/config/capability-flags.ts`, `lib/graph/graph-metadata-parser.ts`, `lib/spec/is-phase-parent.ts`, `lib/graph/access-telemetry.ts`, `lib/resume/resume-ladder.ts`, `lib/validation/generated-metadata-integrity.ts` |
| Generated-metadata grandfather | OFF (enforcing) | `SPECKIT_GENERATED_METADATA_GRANDFATHER` | Graduated default-off-enforcing: a generated-metadata integrity violation (including a missing or mismatched `source_fingerprint`) is a hard strict error. Set `true`/`1` to restore the non-blocking report-mode `info` for a tree that has not been restamped | `lib/config/capability-flags.ts`, `lib/validation/generated-metadata-integrity.ts` |
| Status-completion consistency gate | OFF (report mode) | `SPECKIT_STATUS_COMPLETION_CONSISTENCY_GATE` | New check, inverse polarity from the flag above: cross-checks a stored `derived.status: complete` against `completion_pct`/open `tasks.md` items. Default OFF because a repo-wide sweep found 213 folders already mislabeled `complete` by a prior `deriveStatus` defect (fixed separately). A disagreement surfaces in `--strict` output but stays non-blocking until the existing backlog is reviewed. Set `true`/`1` to enforce (fails strict) | `lib/config/capability-flags.ts`, `lib/validation/generated-metadata-integrity.ts` |
| Identity and merge safety | ON | `SPECKIT_IDENTITY_MERGE_SAFETY` | Graduated default-on: both generators resolve a shared specs-root-relative spec-folder identity and the merge preserves a non-null parent plus unions children so a scoped re-derive cannot erase lineage. Set `false`/`0`/`off` to restore the legacy caller-base path and spread merge | `lib/config/capability-flags.ts`, `lib/graph/graph-metadata-parser.ts` |
| Idempotent description writes | ON | `SPECKIT_IDEMPOTENT_DESCRIPTION_WRITES` | Graduated default-on: a per-folder description write whose only delta is the volatile stamp is skipped and the aggregate-cache write is gated on a real member delta, preserving the prior timestamp. Set `false`/`0`/`off` to restore the unconditional legacy write | `lib/config/capability-flags.ts` |
| Generated-metadata z-exclusion | ON | `SPECKIT_GENERATED_METADATA_Z_EXCLUSION` | Excludes `z-future` staging directories from the spec-folder discovery scanner. `z_archive` stays in the scan. Set `false` to restore the prior scanner that descended them | `lib/search/folder-discovery.ts` |
| Entity config path override | (bundled rules) | `SPECKIT_ENTITY_CONFIG_PATH` | Points `resolveExtractionRules()` at a JSON rule file. A readable, valid file replaces the built-in rules. Any read, parse or validation failure logs a warning and falls back to the built-in set | `lib/extraction/entity-extractor.ts` |
| Metadata disk-path consistency enforce | ON (enforcing) | `SPECKIT_METADATA_DISK_CONSISTENCY_ENFORCE` | Advisory→enforce toggle for the `METADATA_DISK_PATH_CONSISTENCY` rule: a `description.json`/`graph-metadata.json` path mismatch against the on-disk folder fails `--strict`. Graduated 2026-07-10 on a reconciled tree-wide census (1,130 real mismatches reconciled. Remaining residual is non-production paths, see 019-validation-enforce-graduation/implementation-summary.md). Set `false` to fall back to advisory-only | `cli/rules/check-metadata-disk-consistency.sh` |
| Status cross-doc consistency enforce | ON (enforcing) | `SPECKIT_STATUS_CROSS_DOC_ENFORCE` | Advisory→enforce toggle for the `STATUS_CROSS_DOC_CONSISTENCY` rule: a classified-status disagreement between `spec.md` and `implementation-summary.md` fails `--strict`. Graduated 2026-07-10 on a reconciled tree-wide census (2 documented residuals remain, see 019-validation-enforce-graduation/implementation-summary.md). Set `false` to fall back to advisory-only | `cli/rules/check-status-cross-doc-consistency.sh` |
| Graph metadata child-drift enforce | ON (enforcing) | `SPECKIT_CHILD_DRIFT_ENFORCE` | Advisory→enforce toggle for the `GRAPH_METADATA_CHILD_DRIFT` rule: a phase parent's `children_ids` missing an on-disk phase child fails `--strict`. Also gates a dist-presence freshness guard on the child-scanner dependency, so a stale or missing scanner build fails closed rather than silently reporting clean. Graduated 2026-07-10 on a reconciled tree-wide census, see 019-validation-enforce-graduation/implementation-summary.md. Set `false` to fall back to advisory-only | `cli/rules/check-graph-metadata-child-drift.sh`, `cli/lib/dist-freshness.cjs` |

### Provisional Measurement Contract

Publication-facing metric rows now use the shared measurement contract from `lib/context/shared-payload.ts`.

- Every publishable metric field must declare one certainty label: `exact`, `estimated`, `defaulted`, or `unknown`.
- Headline multipliers stay blocked unless prompt, completion, cache-read, and cache-write token fields all have `provider_counted` authority. Later packets should reuse `canPublishMultiplier()` instead of inventing packet-local gates.

[0m$ [0mgrep -n "pivot\|minIterations\|extend\|fan-out\|fanout" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md | head -40
64:`--executor=<kind>` selects the executor kind. The native kind dispatches the agent. The CLI kinds dispatch each round through `dispatch-iteration.cjs`. `cli-mistral-vibe` runs only as a fan-out lineage, so its inline branch fails closed. `--dry-run` previews setup and focus selection, then halts before the first write.
125:`--convergence-mode` changes the stop path. `off` disables convergence STOP while the hard caps stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window. `divergent` sends an eligible legal stop into one bounded pivot round instead of synthesis, then returns to the loop.
127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.
165:`/speckit:plan` and `/speckit:implement` consume the report. `/speckit:resume` rebuilds context before you extend a completed run. `/speckit:save` writes a manual memory save, though a research run already saves at the end.
199:A: `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and appends a `resumed` event. `restart` archives the current `research/` tree under `research_archive/`, mints a fresh `sessionId`, increments `generation` and appends a `restarted` event. `completed-continue` comes from `--extend=N`: it reopens a completed run in the same session, raises the cap to the runs so far plus N, snapshots the prior synthesis and appends an `extended` event.
[0m$ [0mgrep -n "pivot\|minIterations\|extend\|fan-out\|fanout" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md | head -40
141:The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
156:Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.
158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

**[system-skilled-doctor](.skilled/skills/system-skilled-doctor/README.md)** gets Skilled into your own project and keeps it working there. It owns eleven doctor commands for installing, linking, updating and checking a project, including the overlays that carry a copied project's own changes through each update.

**[sk-git](.skilled/skills/sk-git/README.md)** handles worktrees, conventional commits, pull requests and finishing a branch.

**[sk-prompt](.skilled/skills/sk-prompt/README.md)** turns a rough request into a structured, checked prompt.

**[cli-orca](.skilled/skills/cli-orca/README.md)** drives the Orca app's worktrees, terminals, browser and automations.

**[mcp-code-mode](.skilled/skills/mcp-code-mode/README.md)** calls external MCP tools through one TypeScript interface.

Only `sk-code` carries stack-specific patterns, so start there when you adapt Skilled to your own stack. The [sk-code README](.skilled/skills/sk-code/README.md#4-adapting-to-your-stack) walks through it, and `/doctor:sk-code setup` checks the result.

&nbsp;

## 7. 🧩 HOOKS, PLUGINS AND EXTENSIONS

Skilled enforces its rules through hooks that each runtime calls at fixed moments: at session start, on each prompt, around each tool call and at session end. Every hook has one home folder in the shared hook library, with its policy and a README. Each runtime reaches it through a thin adapter in that runtime's own folder, and the [Runtimes](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) table shows where each one loads them.

Runtime support is uneven on purpose, because each runtime exposes different events. The [hooks README](.skilled/hooks/README.md) holds the full coverage matrix. Each hook has its own off switch, and one master switch turns them all off except the git message gate. [Off Switches](#off-switches) names the few you are likely to set.

The hooks below are grouped by purpose. Each entry links the README that holds its runtimes, switches and code.

### Sessions And Continuity

These hooks carry context, goals and decisions from one session to the next.

#### Session Handover

Near the context limit, this hook asks the session to write a handoff note and arms a record for the next one. The skilled-loop wrapper then starts a fresh session in the same terminal, and that session claims the note. Started without the wrapper, Claude Code keeps the note and returns to it after its own auto-compaction.

To start every runtime under the wrapper, add the wrapper to your shell profile. The session handover README shows the line.

The note carries what the next session needs. It holds the goal and what is left. It also names the running command with its mode and arguments. The model, the launch flags and the session name carry over too, and so does the effort on the runtimes that take one at launch. Vibe is the one runtime that keeps no model. The hop waits for the session's background tasks, and your next prompt calls off a hop that is still waiting.

Handover is on by default for Claude Code only. You can turn it on for every runtime at once or for one runtime at a time, and a runtime's own setting wins. Only Claude Code, Codex, Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe nudge and hop on their own. On Cursor and Devin, `/speckit:handoff` writes the note and you start the next session with the printed claim command. One off switch beats every on setting. While handover is off for a runtime, `/speckit:handoff` refuses there and the runtime's own compaction handles a full window.

When the sender ran one of those five runtimes, the session that claims the note can also read the sender's earlier turns back through a printed lookup command, as the [handoff protocol](.skilled/hooks/session-handover/handoff-protocol.md#3-history-lookup-after-a-claim) explains.

Read more in the [session handover README](.skilled/hooks/session-handover/README.md).

[0m$ [0msed -n '14,18p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.11.18.0.md; echo "---v2.1.0 line 25---"; sed -n '23,27p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.0.0.md
# v1.11.18.0, A Review Run Holds Below Its Minimum Iterations

The minimum-iteration floor applied to research runs only. A review that reached a stop candidate before its configured floor could stop early, so the floor now applies to review runs too.

&nbsp;
---v2.1.0 line 25---
- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** A run that is not complete, a fan-out config, or a value that is not a positive integer stops with a named error.

&nbsp;

// iteration counts first and a numeric run is the fallback, as the state reader
            // counts them, because a leaf may write its run id string in run.
            const continuedFromRun = new Set(rows
              .filter((row) => row && row.type === "iteration")
              .map((row) => (Number.isInteger(row.iteration) ? row.iteration : Number(row.run)))
              .filter((run) => Number.isInteger(run))).size;
            const addedIterations = Number(extendRaw);
            const priorMaxIterations = Number(config.maxIterations) || 0;
            const priorMaxDurationMinutes = Number(config.maxDurationMinutes) || 0;
            const createdMs = Date.parse(config.createdAt);
            const elapsedMinutesAtExtension = Number.isFinite(createdMs)
              ? Math.max(0, Math.floor((Date.now() - createdMs) / 60000))
              : 0;
            // An unset flag renders as an empty or unexpanded value, never as digits.
            const hasMinutes = /^[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(String(extendMinutesRaw));
            const addedMinutes = hasMinutes ? Number(extendMinutesRaw) : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            // A run with no duration budget keeps none unless --extend-minutes asks for one.
            const newMaxDurationMinutes = priorMaxDurationMinutes > 0 || hasMinutes
              ? Math.max(priorMaxDurationMinutes, elapsedMinutesAtExtension) + addedMinutes
              : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            const newMaxIterations = continuedFromRun + addedIterations;
            const lineage = config.lineage || {};
            const sessionId = lineage.sessionId;
            const lineageId = lineage.parentSessionId || sessionId;
            const generation = Number(lineage.generation) || 1;
            const event = {
              stem: "deep_research.run_extended",
              scope: { runId: sessionId, lineageId },
              data: {
                priorTailDigest: crypto.createHash("sha256").update(stateBytes).digest("hex"),
                sessionId,
                generation,
                continuedFromRun,
                extension: {
                  addedIterations,
                  priorMaxIterations,
                  newMaxIterations,
                  priorMaxDurationMinutes,
                  newMaxDurationMinutes,
                  elapsedMinutesAtExtension,
                },

# Feature Specification: Playbook coverage, feature catalogs and alignment for the expansive-depth program

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 1 -->

<!-- ANCHOR:phase-context -->
## Phase Context

This is **Phase 14** of the expansive-depth program, and it runs last.

**Scope Boundary**: Documentation only. Operator decision 2026-11-17: each implementation phase writes its own manual testing playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. This phase verifies that coverage, updates the feature catalogs, runs the two alignment checks, fixes the doc findings and reruns. It changes no runtime script, command workflow or agent behavior.

**Dependencies**:
- All of `../001-completed-run-extension/` through `../013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration/` complete, so every documented behavior exists in code and every phase has written its scenarios.

**Deliverables**:
- A coverage check showing every row of the Feature to Playbook Map has a scenario its phase wrote.
- Updated feature catalog entries.
- A clean `/local-doc-alignment` report and a clean `/local-skilled-alignment` report.
<!-- /ANCHOR:phase-context -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:problem -->
## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE
<!-- FILL: State the problem in two sentences: what breaks, and for whom. GOOD: The rotate-logs script never deletes old archives, so a host that copies `logs/archive` fills its disk. WEAK: The script has issues with output. -->

### Problem Statement
Phases 001-013 add `--extend=N`, `--depth-mode=expansive` for deep-research and deep-review, custom review dimensions and defect fixes that change what an operator sees, but no system-deep-loop manual testing playbook or feature catalog mentions any of them. An operator cannot find a scenario that exercises the new flags, and a reviewer cannot check the catalogs against the shipped code.

### Purpose
Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of 001-013 has the playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

<!-- ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->
### Measured Behavior
<!-- FILL: Run the command this change affects and paste the command and its verbatim output. Quote every number the spec states about current behavior from this block. Write `N/A:` and a reason when no command applies. GOOD: `bash scripts/rotate-logs.sh` with 10 archives, then `ls logs/archive | wc -l` prints 10. WEAK: The script deletes old logs. -->
Run on `main` at 2026-11-17 from the repository root (`D=.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop`), before any of 001-013 is built.
```text
$ rg -l -- "--extend=|--depth-mode|depthMode" $D/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook $D/runtime/manual-testing-playbook $D/feature-catalog $D/deep-research/feature-catalog $D/deep-review/feature-catalog $D/runtime/feature-catalog; echo "rg exit=$?"
rg exit=1
$ rg -il "custom review dimension" $D/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-review/feature-catalog; echo "rg exit=$?"
rg exit=1
```
No playbook or catalog file names either flag or custom review dimensions.
<!-- /ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->

<!-- ANCHOR:scope -->
## 3. SCOPE
<!-- FILL: List three to five things this change does, each starting with a verb. Before you list a new file, module, option, layer or dependency, name what fails today without it, or drop it. GOOD: Add a `--keep <n>` option that deletes all but the newest n archives in `logs/archive/`. WEAK: Update the script and docs. -->

### In Scope
- Verify playbook coverage: each row of the Feature to Playbook Map below has a scenario in its category, written by its owning phase through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. A missing scenario is a finding for the owning phase, which adds it through the same command; this phase does not write scenarios.
- Update the system-deep-loop feature catalogs through `/create:feature-catalog system-deep-loop update --path <catalog dir>`.
- Run `/local-doc-alignment` on the expansive-depth topic and `/local-skilled-alignment` on the 001-013 change.
- Fix each doc finding: a playbook gap through its owning phase, a catalog finding through `/create:feature-catalog`, and a finding in a reference doc, `README.md`, `SKILL.md` or command doc by a direct edit here. Regenerate a compiled contract when a fix changes a document it digests. Rerun both checks until neither reports an open finding.

### Out of Scope
- Writing playbook scenarios. Each implementation phase owns its own (operator decision 2026-11-17).
- Runtime, command workflow or agent behavior changes. A finding that shows a behavior bug is a code finding: it is recorded in `goal.md` §4 and handed to the owning phase, which reopens with a fix task. This phase cannot close while a code finding is open, unless the operator records a waiver for it in `goal.md` §4.
- Changelog entries and release notes. Each implementation phase owns its own; `../scratch/cross-phase-handoff.md` lists which phases add one.

### Feature to Playbook Map

Category folders are where each owning phase writes its scenarios. The create command decides the final file names; this phase checks that a file exists in each category.

| Owning phase | User-visible behavior | Playbook category |
|-------|-----------------------|-------------------|
| 001 | `--extend=N` on a completed run | `deep-research/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-review/.../entry-points-and-modes/` |
| 002 | Research on `cli-claude-code` honors the configured Claude config dir | `runtime/.../executor/` |
| 003 | A review run never stops before `minIterations` | `deep-review/.../convergence-and-recovery/` |
| 004 | Next Focus ignores answered free text, review picks an uncovered dimension | `deep-research/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/`, `deep-review/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/` |
| 005 | The research prompt pack allows `research/research.md` and uses `Recommended Next Focus` | `runtime/.../prompt-rendering/` |
| 006 | The `findings_not_enumerated` gate and the `focusAlignment` guard run on real leaf fields | `deep-research/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/` |
| 007 | Source diversity reads derived `quality_class` values | `runtime/.../coverage-graph/` |
| 008 | Custom review dimensions are tracked and can be covered | `deep-review/.../initialization-and-state-setup/`, `deep-review/.../review-depth-v2-rollout/` |
| 009 | A merged fan-out review keeps its coverage surfaces | `deep-review/.../fanout/` |
| 010 | `--depth-mode=expansive` on deep-research | `deep-research/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-research/.../convergence-and-recovery/` |
| 011 | `--depth-mode=expansive` on deep-review | `deep-review/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-review/.../fanout/` |
| 012 | Council seat cap of 3 per attempt and one quorum retry per pivot | `runtime/.../council/` |
| 013 | A landed iteration is not dispatched a second time | `runtime/.../executor/` |

# Cross-phase handoff: work the plan review found unowned

Written 2026-11-17 while applying the Fable 5.2 plan review (`fable-5-2-plan-review.md`) to phases 010, 011 and 014. Those three phases were edited directly. Phases 001-009, 012 and 013 were being edited by other agents at the same time, so nothing in their folders was touched. This file lists what each of those phases has to add so the unowned work in X-6 has an owner.

Shorthand: `S` = `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop`, `C` = `.skilled/commands/deep/assets`.

---

## 1. Operator decision that binds every phase

On 2026-11-17 the operator decided that **each implementation phase writes its own manual testing playbook scenarios** through `/create:manual-testing-playbook system-deep-loop update --path <playbook dir>`. Phase 014 no longer writes scenarios. It checks that each row below has one, updates the feature catalogs and runs the two alignment checks.

Each phase below needs one task: "Write the scenario for this phase's user-visible behavior through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` into the category named here. Verify: the command completes and `rg -l` for the flag or behavior prints a file in that category."

| Phase | Behavior the scenario exercises | Playbook category |
|-------|---------------------------------|-------------------|
| 001 | `--extend=N` on a completed run | `S/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/`, `S/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/` |

| 003 | A review run never stops before `minIterations` | `S/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/` |
| 004 | Next Focus ignores answered free text; review picks an uncovered dimension | `iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/` in both loop playbooks |

[0m$ [0msed -n '125,175p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md
### State on Disk

The packet lives under `{spec_folder}/review/`. A root-spec target writes there directly. A child-phase target writes flat on its first run and gets a `pt-NN` sibling packet only when prior content for a different target already exists.

| File under `{spec_folder}/review/` | Role |
|---|---|
| `deep-review-config.json` | Run settings. Immutable after init, apart from the terminal `status: complete` flip |
| `deep-review-state.jsonl` | Append-only log of iterations, events and stop decisions |
| `deep-review-findings-registry.json` | Reducer-owned index of every finding and its state |
| `deep-review-strategy.md` | Dimension coverage, findings and the next audit focus |
| `deep-review-dashboard.md` | Reducer-generated convergence trend |
| `.deep-review-pause` | Operator-owned pause sentinel |
| `resource-map.md` | Emitted at synthesis unless the run passes `--no-resource-map` |
| `review-report.md` | The nine-section report, written at synthesis |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | One write-once file per iteration |

The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
`fork` is reserved and deferred, so the runtime neither emits nor accepts it. `releaseReadinessState` starts at `in-progress`. It moves to `release-blocking` while any unresolved P0 remains. It moves to `converged` when the run stops converged with a PASS or CONDITIONAL verdict.

### Convergence and the Stop Gates

Convergence is a weighted vote over three signals.

| Signal | Weight | What it measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Rolling average** | 0.30 | Recent severity-weighted finding yield across the last two evidence-bearing iterations |
| **MAD noise floor** | 0.25 | The latest ratio measured against statistical noise |
| **Dimension coverage** | 0.45 | Configured dimensions plus the required traceability protocols are covered, after at least one stabilization pass |

The weighted score has to reach `compositeStopScore`, which defaults to 0.60, before the loop may consider a stop. Clearing the threshold is not enough on its own. Nine binary gates have to pass as well, and a failure writes a `blocked_stop` event with `blockedBy` and a per-gate `gateResults` record. The gate list and its criteria live in `references/convergence/convergence.md`.

Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.

`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

### Fan-out and Merged Verdicts

One run can spread the loop across executor lineages. Pass `--executor` once per lineage, or pass an `--executors` matrix. Cap the pool with `--concurrency`. The merged verdict is strongest restriction: any lineage with an active P0, or a required gate still failing at its stop, turns the merged verdict into FAIL.

---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Use it before a release, when you want a verdict rather than a list of impressions. Use it for a spec folder whose documents have to be checked against the code that ships. Use it for an unattended overnight audit or when each dimension's findings should inform the next pass.

Skip it for a quick one-file check or for a single-pass review of a pull request. `sk-code`'s code-review mode covers those cases faster.
